# Execution Log

Running log of task-by-task execution progress for the Kyro dev task list
(`docs/plan/tasks/M0-tasks.md` … `M7-tasks.md`, `M-CLOUD-tasks.md`). This is a companion to
the inline `[status]` tags used in the task files themselves (see
`docs/plan/tasks/TASKS-INDEX.md` → "Status legend"): the task files record the current status
next to each task title, while this log is the append-only, chronological record of *what
actually happened* each time a task was picked up — which sub-agent ran it, what commit(s) it
produced, and any notes (including test results, and any environment blockers encountered —
cross-reference `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`).

**Convention:** each task's sub-agent appends exactly one row (or more, if it required
multiple sessions/attempts) when it finishes working on that task. Do not edit or delete prior
rows — if a task is revisited, add a new row rather than rewriting history. Keep notes concise;
put detail in the commit message or PR description instead.

| Task ID | Status | Commit(s) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| M0-01 | done | `57e9c9c` | Expo SDK 56 TS app scaffolded at repo root via `create-expo-app` (default/router template) then pinned down from SDK 57 to `~56.0.16` with `expo install expo@^56.0.0` + `expo install --fix` + a clean `pnpm install` (fresh lockfile resolve) to clear all peer-dep conflicts — verified zero peer-dep warnings on final install. `expo-dev-client` and `expo-build-properties` added. Routes moved from the template's default `src/app/` to top-level `app/` per 06 §2 (routes-only, thin screens); template demo content (themed components, tutorial screens, react/expo logos, tab icons) stripped in favor of a minimal placeholder `_layout.tsx`/`index.tsx`; `src/` left present but empty (`.gitkeep`) — feature-folder skeleton is M0-02. `tsconfig.json` came pre-configured by the template with `strict: true` and `@/* -> ./src/*` (plus a bonus `@/assets/* -> ./assets/*`) — verbatim match to spec, no edits needed. `app.json`: name `Kyro`, slug `kyro`, iOS bundle id `com.tejitpabari.kyro`, `newArchEnabled` left unset (SDK 56 default is on). Environment note: `corepack prepare pnpm@latest --activate` failed on this machine's Node 20.20.1 (latest pnpm requires Node ≥22.13); used `corepack prepare pnpm@9 --activate` (pnpm 9.15.9) instead — added to `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`. |

Native-capability seams (08 §5, 06 §10): placeholder `src/lib/{haptics,notifications,keep-awake,files}.ts` (no native packages installed yet — first real consumers are M2/M5) each with a sibling `src/lib/__mocks__/*.ts` manual Jest mock; `src/lib/__tests__/haptics.test.tsx` is the worked example (mock the seam, render a component, assert the seam fires on interaction) and doubles as the acceptance gate's required sample RNTL test. Coverage: `src/data/**` (90/85) and global (75/70) thresholds are active; `src/domain/**` (95/90) and `src/features/workout/**` (85/80) are present as commented-out entries with a `TODO(M1)`/`TODO(M2)` to uncomment once each directory has its first real source file — **this is the one acceptance-gate nuance to flag precisely**: Jest's per-path/glob `coverageThreshold` checker only evaluates a glob if at least one *covered* file matches it; with zero source files under `src/domain/` and `src/features/workout/` today, Jest hard-errors ("Coverage data for `<glob>` was not found") rather than passing vacuously, which is a different failure mode than "below threshold" — confirmed empirically (both with the globs active, seeing the hard error, and by temporarily setting `global` to 99/99 to confirm thresholds truly fail closed, then reverting). Deferring those two entries via comments (values unchanged, ready to uncomment) is exactly the per-glob gating the task spec pre-authorized for this situation; nothing was silently loosened. Deviation: added `expo-sqlite` (`~56.0.5`) as a real dependency (not just a devDependency per the task's literal "install better-sqlite3" line) so `driver.expo.ts` typechecks against real device driver types — full Drizzle/migration wiring is still M0-09's job, this only unblocks the shim's shape. Added `tsconfig.json` `"types": ["jest"]` so global `describe`/`it`/`expect` resolve under `tsc --noEmit`. **Environment blocker hit and resolved** (see `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md` M0-03 update): `better-sqlite3@13.0.1`'s prebuilt binaries are compiled with `NAPI_VERSION=10`, which segfaults inside Node's own `napi_module_register_by_symbol` on this machine's Node v20.20.1 (napi version 9) — reproduced with a minimal from-scratch N-API addon (crash disappears without the `NAPI_VERSION=10` define), root-caused via `gdb` backtrace, confirmed it is not sandbox-related (`dangerouslyDisableSandbox` made no difference) and not fixable by forcing a from-source rebuild (still crashes — the *Node build itself* rejects/mishandles napi 10 modules). Pinned `better-sqlite3` to `12.4.1` (declares Node 20.x support, fetches a compatible prebuild via `prebuild-install`) as the fix, not a workaround-around-the-test — verified the pinned version round-trips real SQLite correctly. `jest-expo` pinned to `56.0.5` (matching the SDK 56 install; `jest-expo@57.x` resolved by default and is for SDK 57) with `@react-native/jest-preset@0.85.3` and `jest@^29` (jest-expo's actual peer range, not the `jest@30` pnpm resolves by default) — clean `pnpm install` with zero peer-dep warnings on a full `rm -rf node_modules && pnpm install`. Verified acceptance gate: `pnpm test` — 2 projects, 3 suites, 8 tests, all green; `pnpm test -- --coverage` — same green, coverage table renders (`src/data/sqlite` 100/100, `src/test/fixtures` 90/87.5, all-files 92.3/90), exits 0; `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm run format:check` all clean on the final tree (also re-verified from a fresh `rm -rf node_modules && pnpm install`). |

(2) pnpm reserves the bare `ci` verb as a built-in command (`clean-install` alias) and shadows any script literally named `ci`; on this machine's pnpm 9.15.9 that built-in isn't implemented, so bare `pnpm ci` exits 1 (`ERR_PNPM_CI_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`) instead of running the script — confirmed this is permanent pnpm CLI behavior (`pnpm ci --help`), not version-specific. The script must be invoked as `pnpm run ci` (verified, exit 0); kept the script name `ci` per the task's literal wording rather than renaming around the collision. |

| M0-05 | done | `e8363ad` | `src/ui/tokens.ts`: complete 07 §2.1–2.5 color tables verbatim (dark + light `bg`/`border`/`text`/`accent`, both-theme `semantic` incl. per-theme danger/warning/info/chartSecondary + shared `overlay`, `setTypeBadgeColors()` mapping W/D/F/normal to those tokens, the 6-color `supersetPalette`, and the §2 intro's `brandRamp` scale reference), 07 §3 `typography` (all 11 named styles — display/title1/title2/title3/headline/body/subhead/footnote/caption/statLarge/statSmall/setValue — exact size/weight, `fontVariant: ['tabular-nums']` on statLarge/statSmall/setValue only), and 07 §4 `spacing` (4pt-multiple scale keyed `'0.5'…'12'` matching the doc's `space.05–12` naming, plus `layout.{screenGutter,cardPadding,cardGap}` aliases) + `radii` (sm/md/lg/pill). Alpha-blended tokens (`bg.accentSubtle`, `overlay`) are stored as `{hex, opacity}` (`AlphaOverlay`) plus flattened to an `rgba()` string for direct RN consumption — `alphaOverlayToRgba`/`compositeOverlayOverHex` are pure helpers so the contrast test can composite the *effective* on-screen color over an arbitrary background. `tokens.ts` has **zero React/React Native imports** (verified: `pnpm lint` clean, and manually confirmed no `react`/`react-native`/`expo*` import exists in the file). `src/ui/theme-provider.tsx` (separate file, the one allowed to depend on React/RN): `ThemeProvider` + `useTheme()` context; resolves `ThemePreference` (`system\|light\|dark`) via `useColorScheme` when `system`, falling back to `dark` (07 §1 dark-first) when the OS scheme is unavailable. **Integration seam for M0-10** (documented in a file-header comment in `theme-provider.tsx`): the provider is a controlled component with an uncontrolled fallback — pass `preference`+`onPreferenceChange` for the M0-10 shape (app root reads `settingsStore.theme`, wires `onPreferenceChange` to `SettingsRepository.set('theme', …)`); leave both undefined (today) and it manages its own local `useState<ThemePreference>('system')` so M0-06 through M0-09 have a working theme before `SettingsRepository` exists. Contrast test (`src/ui/__tests__/tokens.test.ts`, real WCAG relative-luminance/contrast-ratio math, not a hardcoded pass — sanity-checked against black-on-white = 21:1) covers every 07 §2.6 pairing for **both** themes: text.primary ≥7:1 on all 3 backgrounds, text.secondary ≥4.5:1 on all 3, accent.text ≥4.5:1 on bg.base/bg.surface, accent.onAccent ≥3:1 on accent.primary and accent.pressed, and bg.accentSubtle composited over bg.base/bg.surface keeps both text.primary and text.secondary ≥4.5:1. **Actual computed ratios** (worst-case per requirement, both themes clear their threshold with margin): dark text.primary/bg.elevated 14.63:1 (≥7 required); light text.secondary/bg.elevated 5.52:1 (≥4.5); light accent.text/bg.base 5.04:1 (≥4.5); light accent.onAccent/accent.primary 3.77:1 (≥3, the tightest margin in the whole table); dark text.secondary on accentSubtle-over-surface 6.35:1 (≥4.5, the tightest checked-row-tint margin). Routing: this test runs in the fast `node` Jest project (added a scoped `testMatch` entry in `jest.config.js` for exactly this one file, and a matching `testPathIgnorePatterns` entry in the `ui` project so it isn't double-run under jest-expo) since `tokens.ts` has no RN dependency. `src/ui/__tests__/theme-provider.test.tsx` (RNTL, `ui` project) covers: controlled light/dark resolution, re-render on `preference` prop change (the literal acceptance-gate case — asserts a token-consuming test component's rendered `backgroundColor` style changes), `useTheme()` outside a provider throws, all three `system` resolution branches (OS light/dark/unavailable-fallback) via `jest.spyOn(ReactNative, 'useColorScheme')` (patches the already-initialized module's export in place — `jest.mock('react-native', factory)` was tried first and breaks jest-expo's native module registry, see below), uncontrolled local-state mode + `setPreference`, and controlled mode correctly *not* self-updating from its own `setPreference` call. One TS-type wrinkle worth flagging for future RN-mocking code in this repo: this project's resolved `react-native` type declarations (`node_modules/react-native/types/index.d.ts`, selected via the `expo/tsconfig.base` `moduleResolution: "bundler"` + `customConditions: ["react-native"]` combo) type `useColorScheme()`'s return as bare `ColorSchemeName` with **no `null`/`undefined`** in the exported public types — even though the real native implementation (and the `types_generated/` internal declarations) can return `null`. Worked around with a single explicit cast at the test's one mock-seam helper (`mockSystemColorScheme`), not by loosening `theme-provider.tsx`'s own runtime-safe fallback logic (`systemScheme === 'light' \|\| systemScheme === 'dark' ? … : FALLBACK_THEME` already handles `null`/`undefined`/`'unspecified'` correctly at runtime regardless of what TS believes). `migrate(driver, migrations = MIGRATIONS)` takes an injectable migrations list purely for test determinism (out-of-order sort, empty-list edge cases) without touching the real manifest. **DB-open wiring** (`src/data/sqlite/open-database.ts`): app-only, opens via the existing `driver.expo.ts` shim (M0-03, untouched) and turns WAL mode on via `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL` (better-sqlite3's driver already sets this itself, M0-03). **Boot seam** (`src/data/sqlite/boot.ts`): `runDbBoot()` opens the app db + runs `migrate()`, exposes the live connection via `getAppDriver()` for M0-10+ to reuse rather than opening a second connection; Diffed both commits in full (`git show da8a195 d1dd08d --stat` + full diffs) against M0-09's spec, 05 §3.5/§10, and 06 §5.1/§9. **Migration scope (critical check, spec §3):** read the actual generated `src/data/migrations/0000_app_meta_and_settings.sql` directly, not just `schema.ts` — confirmed it contains **exactly** `CREATE TABLE app_meta (key text PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, value text NOT NULL)` and `CREATE TABLE settings (key text PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, value text NOT NULL)`, nothing else; `meta/0000_snapshot.json`'s `tables` object likewise lists only these two. No scope creep into the full v1 schema — M1-01's migration 0002 is untouched territory. **Babel/manifest pattern extends cleanly (spec §4):** `manifest.ts`'s `MIGRATIONS` is a plain ordered array (`{version, tag, sql}` entries); adding migration 0002 is purely additive — one new `import` + one new array entry with `version: 2` — nothing in `manifest.ts`, `babel.config.js`, or `jest.config.js`'s mirrored plugin config is hardcoded to a single-migration count (no `MIGRATIONS.length === 1` assumption, no migration-count-specific logic anywhere in `migrator.ts` either — `migrate()` sorts by `version` and filters `> fromVersion` generically). `babel-plugin-inline-import` is scoped to `.sql` generically via `extensions: ['.sql']`, not to a specific filename. Root `babel.config.js` addition confirmed behaviorally inert for pre-existing files (matches Metro's prior implicit `babel-preset-expo` fallback). **Idempotency/transactionality (spec §5):** read `migrator.ts` — each migration's statements + the `schema_version` upsert run inside one `driver.transaction()` call; `driver.better-sqlite3.ts`'s `transaction()` uses `db.transaction(fn)()` (real rollback-on-throw). `migrator.test.ts`'s idempotency test genuinely re-invokes `migrate()` a second time on an already-migrated db and asserts `applied: []`/unchanged versions — not a single-call test dressed up. A separate test forces a mid-migration failure (pre-existing colliding `settings` table) and asserts only `settings` exists afterward (not `app_meta`), proving the transaction actually rolls back the first statement too. **`schema_version` correctness (spec §6):** manifest's `version: 1` (the app's own 1-based counter, independent of drizzle-kit's `0000`-numbered file) is what lands in `app_meta.schema_version`; `migrator.test.ts` asserts `SELECT value FROM app_meta WHERE key='schema_version'` equals `'1'` after migrating from empty — no off-by-one, and two more tests separately pin the version-0 bootstrap fallback (table absent, and table present but no/unparseable row). **DB-ready gate Jest mocking (spec §7):** `db-gate.test.tsx` and the updated `tabs-layout.test.tsx` both use a **factory** `jest.mock('@/data/sqlite/boot', () => ({runDbBoot: jest.fn(), getAppDriver: jest.fn()}))`, not bare `jest.mock(...)` — verified this is the correct approach (bare automocking still `require`s the real module first to infer shape, which would transitively load `driver.expo.ts` → `expo-sqlite`'s native module and fail under Jest); confirmed no test in this diff imports `open-database.ts`/`driver.expo.ts` directly. **Independent full re-verification:** `pnpm run ci` end-to-end green — `tsc`/`eslint`/`prettier` clean, 23 suites/155 tests, coverage 93.55%/89.83%/79.81%/94.06% (src/data/** 100%/100%), `expo-doctor` 21/21, `expo export --platform ios` 3501 modules — all numbers match the commit's reported figures exactly. No blocking issues found. Verdict: **pass, clean**. |

Header comment documents the M2 logger-route requirement verbatim (06 §9's parenthetical: reset must never tear down `activeWorkoutStore`, since remounting re-renders from whatever the store currently holds, already write-through-persisted to SQLite) for whoever wraps that route once it exists. **Per-tab wiring**: added `app/(tabs)/{workout,history,exercises,profile}/_layout.tsx` — each wraps `<Slot />` in `<ErrorBoundary boundaryName="tab:<name>" onError={reportBoundaryError}>` (expo-router's idiomatic segment-layout pattern; `profile`'s boundary also covers the nested `profile/settings` route for free). No route/screen files touched. **`src/lib/logger.ts`**: `Logger` class, pure/dependency-free, ring buffer of `LOG_RING_BUFFER_CAPACITY = 500` events (`{timestamp, level, message}`), `record()`/`debug()`/`info()`/`warn()`/`error()` push-and-evict-oldest-on-overflow, `exportEvents()` returns a chronological (oldest-first) snapshot copy — the seam for the future M5-04 diagnostics screen. **Persistence simplification (explicitly permitted by the task):** `LogPersistence` is an injectable interface (`load`/`save`) defaulting to `noopLogPersistence` (nothing survives relaunch); a real `expo-sqlite/kv-store`-backed implementation was deliberately **not** built — confirmed `expo-sqlite/kv-store` exists in the installed package (`node_modules/expo-sqlite@56.0.5`'s `./kv-store` export, backed by `SQLiteStorage`/`openDatabaseSync`) but, like `src/data/sqlite/driver.expo.ts`, it needs a native module host and cannot be `require`d by a Jest test (jest-expo ships no expo-sqlite mock) — a real implementation belongs in its own device-only file, mirroring the existing `driver.expo.ts`/`driver.better-sqlite3.ts` split, and is deferred to whichever task first needs logs to survive a relaunch (at the latest M5-04). DSN empty string → not called; DSN present → called once with `{dsn, sendDefaultPii:false}`; idempotent on a second call; breadcrumb truncates at the first newline; `captureError` forwards to `captureException`. `src/lib/__tests__/logger.test.ts` (9 tests) — chronological ordering with non-decreasing timestamps, the literal 501st-event-evicts-`event-0` overflow case (asserted both buffer length staying at 500 and the exact surviving oldest/newest messages), `exportEvents()` snapshot-not-reference semantics, `clear()`, per-level tagging, best-effort `persistence.save()` (asserted not to throw or drop state when `save` rejects), `hydrate()` capping to capacity oldest-first, and the default singleton's no-op persistence confirmed via `hydrate()` yielding an empty buffer. `src/lib/__tests__/error-reporting.test.ts` (1 test) — `reportBoundaryError` writes one `error`-level log line and forwards to `Sentry.captureException`. `src/ui/__tests__/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` (6 RNTL tests) — renders children normally when nothing throws; Fix: set `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` explicitly on every open, on both backends. Pure TS, zero React/RN/Expo imports, confirmed by the pre-existing `src/domain/**` ESLint boundary zone (`eslint.config.js`, from M0-02/M1-01) staying clean (`pnpm lint` exit 0). **API design decision — raw-conversion vs. display-rounded split:** implemented exactly the two-tier shape the task itself suggested, made explicit in the file's header comment so later tasks don't have to reverse-engineer it. Tier 1: raw, full-precision, round-trip-safe conversions with no rounding — `kgToLb`/`lbToKg`, `mToKm`/`kmToM`, `mToMiles`/`milesToM`, `mToFeet`/`feetToM`, `cmToIn`/`inToCm` — these are what 05 §5's "no drift rule" needs (conversions only at the display/input boundary, canonical storage stays exact) and what M1-02's own round-trip stability test exercises. Tier 2: `format*`-prefixed functions (`formatWeightLb`, `formatDistanceKm`, `formatDistanceMiles`, `formatDistanceFeet`, `formatLengthIn`) that compose the matching raw conversion with a rounding step and return a **rounded number** (not a string — string/locale presentation is left to the UI layer). Rounding rule for weight is evaluated on the converted lb magnitude (nearest 0.5 lb if `abs(lb) >= 10`, else nearest 0.1 lb) per 05 §5's literal wording. `formatDuration`/`parseTimeInput` don't fit this split (seconds are already canonical and exact; mm:ss/h:mm:ss is pure string presentation) so they're standalone. Later tasks (M2-06 set table, M2-16 warm-up calc) should reach for the raw functions when a result feeds back into further math/storage, and `format*` only at final render. **mm:ss parse-vs-normalize interpretation (08 §4.5 / TASKS-INDEX.md item 6) — read this before touching `parseTimeInput`:** the two named cases ("130" → 90 s digit-fill parse, and bare "90" → 1:30/90 s normalize) read like they could be two different code paths, but they resolve to **one formula**, not two: strip non-digits from the buffer, read the remainder as a plain integer `digits`, then `seconds = digits % 100` (the rightmost up-to-2 digits, taken literally even when that chunk is itself >= 60 — e.g. "90"'s chunk is 90) and `minutes = floor(digits / 100)`, `total = minutes*60 + seconds`. This is algebraically identical to first carrying the seconds overflow into minutes (`minutes*60+seconds` and `(minutes+floor(seconds/60))*60 + seconds%60` are always equal), so no separate carry step exists in the code — `"130"` → digits=130 → sec=30,min=1 → 90 s; `"90"` → digits=90 → sec=90,min=0 → 90 s. **Both land on exactly 90 s**, matching 08 §4.5's own worked numbers for both cases, and `formatDuration(90)` renders either as `"1:30"` — so the "bare seconds >= 60 normalize" case is just `formatDuration` applied to `parseTimeInput`'s output, not a distinct parse rule. Documented at length in `parseTimeInput`'s own doc comment so a future reader isn't tempted to "fix" this into two branches. Passthrough: `null` → `null`; empty string (buffer typed into but no digits yet) → `0` (distinct from `null`, matching a TIME cell that's been focused but not typed into). **0-and-null passthrough:** every exported function checks `=== null` explicitly (never a falsy check, which would mistreat `0`) and returns `null` for `null` in; `0` in always produces a real computed value, never `NaN`/short-circuited — covered by a dedicated test block enumerating every export. **Tests** (`src/domain/__tests__/units.test.ts`, 27 cases): kg↔lb round-trip stability via a seeded `mulberry32` PRNG (deterministic, no `Math.random`) — 100 random kg values × 3 round trips and 100 random lb values × 3 round trips, both asserting drift `< 1e-6`; display rounding rules for weight (both sides of the 10 lb boundary), km/miles (2 decimals), feet (whole number), inches (1 decimal); miles/km and cm/in raw formulas checked against 05 §5's literal constants; duration formatting mm:ss/h:mm:ss including the exact 1 h boundary (3599 → `"59:59"`, 3600 → `"1:00:00"`) and a defensive negative-duration case; the full mm:ss parse suite including the "130"/"90" pair, single/double/four-digit fills, non-digit stripping, and empty-string; and the 0-and-null passthrough block. **Verification:** `pnpm test -- src/domain/__tests__/units.test.ts` 27/27 green; `pnpm test -- --coverage` (full project) 269/269 green (36 suites) — `src/domain/units.ts` **100%/100%/100%/100%** stmt/branch/func/line (clears the 95/90 `src/domain/**` threshold that M1-01 already activated in `jest.config.js` — confirmed that entry was pre-uncommented by M1-01, so no jest.config.js change was needed this task, contrary to the task text's literal "uncomment" instruction which had already been superseded), `src/data/**` and global thresholds unaffected and still clearing their floors; `tsc --noEmit` and `pnpm lint` (`eslint .`) both exit 0 on the full project, confirming the domain-boundary lint zone accepts the new file with zero exceptions needed. No blockers, nothing deferred. |

Diacritic-insensitive alias search: `domain/search.ts`'s `matchesSearchQuery` unit tests already cover both directions (accented haystack/plain query and plain haystack/accented query) at the domain level; added two more integration tests directly against `ExerciseRepositoryImpl.list`'s alias path specifically (accented query "cúrl" → plain-ASCII alias "Curl"; plain-ASCII query "uberkopfdrucken" → accented alias "Überkopfdrücken") since the existing repo-level alias tests only exercised the plain-ASCII/plain-ASCII case — both pass, confirming `normalizeForSearch` really is applied symmetrically to both sides in the repository, not just in the pure `domain/search.ts` unit tests. Also simplified `list`'s query-filter implementation to actually call `matchesSearchQuery` (it was re-deriving the identical normalize-and-`.includes()` logic inline, which the file's own header comment already claimed — now the code matches the doc, no behavior change). **Blocking issue found and fixed (`d25ef75`): the UUID generation strategy.** The implementer's `create()` used `crypto.randomUUID()` with a `Math.random()`-based v4 fallback, flagging it as "unconfirmed" whether on-device Hermes exposes `crypto.randomUUID`. Investigated directly rather than leaving it flagged: grepped the installed `react-native` package's entire source for `randomUUID`/`getRandomValues` — no JS-facing Web-Crypto polyfill exists anywhere in it (the only hits are unrelated native-side `randomUUID()` calls inside Android's own networking/blob modules, never exposed to JS), so `globalThis.crypto` is confirmed `undefined` on-device. That means the `Math.random` fallback was not a rare degraded mode — it was the *only* code path that could ever execute in a real build, for every exercise a user ever creates, and `Math.random` gives no real uniqueness guarantee (a real, if currently latent, data-integrity risk once MC-03's idempotent cloud-sync UUID upserts land, `12` §5/§9). Confirmed the right fix is Expo's own first-party module: installed `expo-crypto@~56.0.4` (matches the existing `expo-*` SDK 56 pin style; not previously listed in `06` §1's dependency table, but matches its own stated rationale — "first-party modules for every native need" — for exactly this need) and verified its `randomUUID()` is a real, synchronous, CSPRNG-backed implementation (unpacked the published tarball and read `Crypto.d.ts` directly rather than trusting the package description). Per the task's own instruction to consider a shared location since `WorkoutRepository`/`RoutineRepository` (M2/M3) need the identical primitive: extracted `src/data/shared/uuid.ts` exporting `generateUuid(): Promise<string>` — prefers `globalThis.crypto.randomUUID()` (confirmed always present in the Node/Jest process both Jest projects run in — Node has shipped this since Node 19, verified empirically against this repo's Node 20 runtime), else a **deferred** `await import('expo-crypto')` (not a top-level import) so the module stays importable from the fast `node` Jest project, which has no `jest-expo` preset/native-module host at all — a top-level `expo-crypto` import would call `requireNativeModule` at module-evaluation time and throw there. **Zone placement reasoning (also documented in the new file's header for future readers):** not `src/domain/` (its ESLint zone rule hard-blocks any `expo-*` import) and not `src/lib/` (`eslint.config.js`'s `import/no-restricted-paths` for the `src/data` zone explicitly forbids `src/data` importing `src/lib` — 06 §2's "data imports domain types only" boundary is lint-enforced, not just documented) — `src/data/shared/` is a same-zone import for every current and future `src/data/<table>/` repository, crossing no boundary. Updated `ExerciseRepositoryImpl.create()` to `await generateUuid()` and deleted the old local `generateExerciseId`/`Math.random` function entirely. Wrote `src/data/shared/__tests__/uuid.test.ts` covering both branches via `jest.mock('expo-crypto', ...)` (the real native module is never touched, matching `driver.expo.ts`'s established "device-only code is not Jest-testable, tested via a mock/smoke-test instead" precedent) and removed the exercise-repository suite's now-obsolete "falls back to a locally-generated uuid v4" test (that fallback branch is `expo-crypto` now, exercised in `uuid.test.ts` instead). **Independent full re-verification (post-fix):** `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` clean; `pnpm test -- --coverage` 436/436 green (41 suites; was 432 — net +4: 3 new `uuid.test.ts` cases + 2 new alias-symmetry cases − 1 removed obsolete case), `src/data/**` 100% lines/95.28% branches (clears 90/85), `src/domain/**` untouched at 100%/100% (clears 95/90), global 97.15%/94.31%/89.61%/97.24% (clears 75/70); `npx expo-doctor` 21/21 (confirms the new `expo-crypto` dependency is wired correctly, no config-plugin/autolinking issue). **`WorkoutRepository`/`RoutineRepository` (M2/M3) note:** import `generateUuid` from `src/data/shared/uuid.ts` (signature: `generateUuid(): Promise<string>`) for id generation — do not re-derive a local UUID generator. **Muscle filter semantics call** (implementer's note, unchanged by this review): `list`'s `muscle` filter matches `primaryMuscleGroup` only — still unconfirmed against product intent, carried forward unresolved. Verdict: **pass, with one blocking fix** (weak/effectively-always-active `Math.random` UUID fallback on-device; corrected to a real CSPRNG-backed `expo-crypto` fallback behind a shared, DI-free `src/data/shared/uuid.ts` primitive; independently tested and verified). |
| M1-07 | done | `bfc0342` | Real Exercises-tab browse screen (03 §2), replacing the M0-08 placeholder. `src/features/exercises/ExerciseBrowseScreen.tsx` composes existing `src/ui/` primitives only: header + "+" create button, `SearchBar` debounced 150 ms (06 §8) over a single `ExerciseRepository.list()` preload (no per-keystroke re-query), `Chip`-opened `FilterOptionSheet.tsx` (single-select equipment/muscle sheets, `Clear` resets, active accent tint), a `Recent` section (`recentlyUsed(10)`, rendered only when non-empty and only while unfiltered per 03 §2), an alphabetical `All` section with sticky per-letter headers + a right-edge `AzRail.tsx` (26-letter index, disabled for letters with no current section, wired to `FlashListRef.scrollToIndex`), and a "no matches" empty state with a `Create "{query}"` shortcut. Filtering/row-building logic lives in a pure, RN-free `exercise-list-model.ts` (`filterExercises` AND-composes query+muscle+equipment; `buildExerciseRows` builds the flat sticky-header+row array) — unit-tested directly against the real, fully-seeded 873-row dataset (`exercise-list-model.test.ts`, 17 cases). **FlashList v2 (installed `@shopify/flash-list@2.3.2`) API differences from v1, relevant to this task's "fixed row height"/`estimatedItemSize` acceptance wording:** v2 is a from-scratch, new-architecture-only rewrite with **no `estimatedItemSize` prop at all** — it auto-measures every item, no size estimate accepted or needed (confirmed by reading `FlashListProps.d.ts`, and the package's own README: "doesn't require any estimates... moving to a JS-only solution"). The task's acceptance gate ("confirm FlashList is configured with fixed estimatedItemSize") is written against v1's API; satisfied it in spirit instead — `ExerciseRow`/`SectionHeaderRow` both render at genuinely constant, non-computed heights (`EXERCISE_ROW_HEIGHT = 64`, `SECTION_HEADER_HEIGHT = 32`, exported constants, asserted equal to those literals in `ExerciseBrowseScreen.full-dataset.test.tsx`) — still the real perf lever 06 §8 is after, just not gated by a prop that no longer exists in the installed version. `stickyHeaderIndices` (used for the letter headers) is inherited from RN's `ScrollViewProps` (`FlashListProps extends Omit<ScrollViewProps, 'maintainVisibleContentPosition'>`), not a FlashList-specific prop — confirmed present and functional. New-arch-only is a non-issue here: this app has no `newArchEnabled` override in `app.json`, meaning it's on Expo SDK 56's new-architecture-only default already. **Built-in thumbnail rendering — a real gap the M1-04/M1-05 pipeline left open, closed here, not deferred:** `images[]` on every built-in is an asset-key string (`"exercises/{id}/0.jpg"`) relative to `assets/`, but Metro has no `require.context`/dynamic-path `require(...)` — a row can't `require()` an image keyed by a value only known at render time. Added `scripts/generate-exercise-thumbnail-registry.ts` (reads `assets/exercise-db.json`, writes `src/features/exercises/exercise-thumbnail-registry.generated.ts`, one static `require(...)` per built-in id's 128 px `thumb.jpg`, 873 entries, ~3.6 MB total — small enough to be an unconditional bundle cost) + `npm run build:exercise-thumbnails` to regenerate it after any future dataset id-set change (M1-11 curation, etc.) — does not touch/modify M1-04's `build-exercise-db.ts` or M1-05's seed migration at all, purely an additive runtime-resolution layer (`exercise-thumbnail.ts`'s `resolveExerciseThumbnailSource`) that reads the generated registry for built-ins and returns `undefined` (Avatar/Thumb's initial-letter fallback) for anything else, including every custom exercise today (`src/lib/files.ts`'s on-device photo storage is still M1-09's stub — customs cannot have a non-empty `images[]` yet, so that branch is provably unreached until M1-09 lands, not just assumed). **Two navigation seams deliberately wired to non-existent routes, per this task's own instructions** ("wire the intended navigation call... don't build a fake target screen"): (1) `router.push({ pathname: '/exercise/new', params: { name } })` — both the empty-search "Create "{query}"" shortcut (name pre-filled from the trimmed query) and the header "+" button (no `name` param) — **M1-09's create-custom-exercise form is the route that must exist at this path**; (2) `router.push(\`/exercise/\${exercise.id}\`)` on every row tap — **M1-08's exercise detail screen** is the route. Both `router.push` calls are asserted via a mocked `expo-router` in `ExerciseBrowseScreen.test.tsx` (exact pathname + params). **Real, non-trivial test-infra issue found and fixed while building the RNTL suite, not swept under the rug:** `ExerciseBrowseScreen` is this codebase's first `useQuery` (`@tanstack/react-query`) consumer (06 §4: "read models stay pure TanStack-Query-over-SQLite") — isolated repro during this task proved a bare `QueryClientProvider` + one active `useQuery` (no FlashList, no SQLite, 1-item data) reliably left the Jest worker process alive indefinitely after every test passed ("Jest did not exit one second after the test run has completed", confirmed via `time` showing near-zero CPU during the hang — a genuinely parked process, not slow). Root cause: a `QueryClient`'s default `gcTime` (5 min) schedules a real `setTimeout` once a query becomes unobserved, and `QueryClientProvider`'s own unmount only tears down its focus/online listeners, never that per-query GC timer. Fix (confirmed via the same isolated repro): every test-owned `QueryClient` in this feature's suites sets `gcTime: 0` in `defaultOptions.queries` — test-only, does not touch `app/_layout.tsx`'s real app `QueryClient`. **A second, unrelated RNTL pitfall found the same way:** `@testing-library/react-native` v14's `fireEvent.press`/`fireEvent.changeText` are `async` (each wraps its handler call in `act()` and returns that promise) — chaining un-awaited `fireEvent.press` calls (e.g. select a muscle-sheet option, then immediately press the equipment chip) let the second interaction start before the first one's state update had flushed, intermittently leaving a filter sheet "stuck open" with the selection never applied even though the underlying `onChange`/`onDismiss` callbacks really did fire (confirmed via a temporary debug log in `FilterOptionSheet`). Fixed by `await`-ing every `fireEvent.*` call in this feature's test suites — documented at length in `ExerciseBrowseScreen.test.tsx`'s header so the next test file in this codebase using `fireEvent` inside a multi-step interaction doesn't lose time rediscovering it. **Test split rationale:** the full real 873-row dataset + real `FlashList` is exercised exactly once, in `ExerciseBrowseScreen.full-dataset.test.tsx` (the "no crash/hang, all 873 rows reachable in the data layer" acceptance evidence — `repository.list()` asserted at length 873, then the whole screen mounted against it without throwing); every other behavioral case (`ExerciseBrowseScreen.test.tsx`, 12 cases) uses `exercise-fixtures.ts`'s small, hand-picked subset of **real** records (verbatim field values copied from `assets/exercise-db.json` — "Barbell Bench Press - Medium Grip" vs. "Dumbbell Bench Press", "Barbell Squat"/"Zercher Squats" vs. "Bodyweight Squat"/"Dumbbell Squat", etc.) plus a lightweight `@shopify/flash-list` mock (renders every row through the screen's real `renderItem`, exposes `scrollToIndex` as a spy) — real per-row rendering/filtering logic, no FlashList virtualization/measurement flakiness. Covers: search+equipment AND-compose (known 32→subset behavior spot-checked via 3 concrete exercises), search+muscle+equipment triple AND-compose, `Clear`, A–Z rail `scrollToIndex` call (correct target index) and disabled-letter no-op, empty-search Create-shortcut navigation (with and without a query), row-tap navigation, custom-exercise-with-no-images placeholder thumbnail (`within(row)`-scoped since the A–Z rail also renders a bare "M" letter elsewhere on screen), Recent-section visibility toggling, and a zero-exercises edge case (empty state renders, no Create CTA without a query). `AzRail.test.tsx` unit-tests the rail component itself in isolation (available vs. disabled letters). Updated `app/__tests__/tabs-layout.test.tsx`'s stale "Exercise library coming soon" placeholder-text assertion to the new screen's real empty-state text (its mocked `getAppDriver`/`queryAll` returns `[]` for everything, so the Exercises route legitimately renders the empty state in that smoke test). **`jest.config.js` change:** the `ui` project's `transformIgnorePatterns` is now spelled out explicitly (was previously just `jest-expo`'s untouched default) with one addition, `@shopify/flash-list` — its `dist/index.js` is plain ESM, which `jest-expo`'s default pattern doesn't un-ignore; the added pattern is `jest-expo`'s own default list plus that one alternative, not a hand-rolled replacement, so every other package (`.pnpm` layout, `expo*`, `react-navigation`, etc.) that preset already handled keeps working. **Verified:** `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` (`eslint .`) clean; full `pnpm test -- --coverage` — **45 suites / 469 tests green**, all exit cleanly (no hang) including the full-dataset FlashList test; `src/domain/**`/`src/data/**`/global coverage thresholds all hold (global 98.19%/93.76%/89.04%/98.3% stmt/branch/func/line); `src/features/exercises/**` (not yet a tracked threshold glob) at 97.77%/90.54% lines/branches. Nothing else deferred within this task's scope; picker mode (multi-select sheet, superset toggle, "Add N" button) is explicitly out of scope per the task text (M2-09). |

| M2-02 | done | `0c7a5ed` | Extends `WorkoutRepositoryImpl` (M2-01, `52195dc`) with the granular mutators + `previousSets` (05 §6 / M2-02). New methods: `addExercises`, `removeExercise`, `reorderExercises`, `replaceExercise`, `addSet`, `updateSet`, `removeSet`, `setSetType`, `setCompleted`, `updateExercise`, `updateMeta`, `previousSets`. `WorkoutRepositoryImpl` now `implements WorkoutRepository` (the merged `WorkoutRepositoryLifecycle & WorkoutRepositoryMutators` interface from `types.ts`), and the M2-02-only error classes (`WorkoutExerciseNotFoundError`/`SetNotFoundError`/`ReorderMismatchError`) that were deliberately deferred out of the M2-01 commit (per that entry's coverage-gate note) landed here now that the methods throwing them exist. **`addExercises`:** pre-creates the same number of bare (`set_type='normal'`, every value field `NULL`, unchecked) set rows as the exercise's most recent completed occurrence had, or 1 if no history — count only, per 02 §3's "same number of set rows ... with previous values as placeholders": placeholders are a display concept computed live by `previousSets`, never written to the new rows. Reads (history-count lookup) and id generation happen before the single `driver.transaction(...)` covering the whole batch, since `generateUuid` is async and the transaction callback must be sync (mirrors `better-sqlite3`'s own contract) — tested for atomicity: a forced failure on the *second* item's INSERT rolls back the *first* item's insert too, proving the whole call — not per-item — is the transaction boundary. **Position renumbering:** `removeExercise`/`removeSet` each renumber their siblings back to contiguous 0-based positions in the same transaction as the delete; `reorderExercises` validates the supplied id list is an exact permutation of the workout's current exercise ids before applying it (throws `ReorderMismatchError` otherwise — missing id, extra id, and duplicate-id cases all tested). **`replaceExercise`:** repoints `exercise_id`, keeps every set row's `position` (count preserved), resets `set_type` to `'normal'`, clears `is_completed` and every value field — `notes`/`restSeconds`/`supersetId` deliberately left untouched (conservative reading of "keeps set count, clears values/placeholders" — 02 §3 doesn't mention those three). **`updateSet`/`updateExercise`/`updateMeta`:** same undefined-vs-null patch convention `ExerciseRepositoryImpl.update` established (M1-06) — a key's absence means "unchanged," an explicit `null` clears a nullable field; extended here to numeric/nullable value fields (`weightKg`, `restSeconds`, `supersetId`, `description`, `endTime`) rather than just strings. **`previousSets(exerciseId, {routineId?, beforeWorkoutId?, occurrenceIndex?})`:** finds the single most recent completed workout containing `exerciseId` (optional `routineId` restriction; optional `beforeWorkoutId` — "at or before that workout's `start_time`, excluding it," for the future edit-in-place case, M2-15/`02` §15; an unresolvable `beforeWorkoutId` degrades to no restriction rather than throwing, since this is a read helper), then the `occurrenceIndex`-th `workout_exercises` row for that exercise within that one workout (0-based, default 0), then splits that occurrence's sets into two **independently-numbered buckets** (warm-up, non-warm-up) in `position` order — matching 02 §6's "warm-up rows reference the previous session's warm-up sets by warm-up index." **`occurrenceIndex` is a deliberate addition on top of 05 §6's stated `{routineId?; beforeWorkoutId?}` shape** (that section frames itself as "verbatim signatures, simplified") — without it there is no way to implement 02 §16.6's "duplicate exercise twice in one workout ... PREVIOUS matches by occurrence order," tested directly (two occurrences of the same exercise in one fixture workout resolve to different previous values by `occurrenceIndex`). No `is_completed` filter needed in the SQL: every set in a genuinely *completed* workout is `is_completed=1` by the `finish` invariant (M2-01), so "checked" is automatic. A previous session with fewer sets than the current workout's rows simply yields fewer array entries (no padding) — tested explicitly; downstream display fallback to `—` is M2-04's concern, not this repository's. Reuses `idx_we_exercise` + `idx_workouts_start` (both already present from M1-01/05 §3.2 — no new migration needed, confirmed by reading `schema.ts` before starting). `addExercises`'s history-count lookup shares the exact same private helper `previousSets` calls (`findMostRecentOccurrenceSetRows`), called with `occurrenceIndex: 0` and no routine/before restriction — one source of truth. **Tests:** 33 new integration tests (`better-sqlite3`, real migrated schema, same raw-SQL-fixture pattern as M2-01's suite) — one `describe` per mutator including position-renumbering assertions, the atomicity test described above, and previousSets' any_workout / same_routine / occurrence-matching / fewer-previous-sets / beforeWorkoutId / no-history cases. **CI:** ran the full `pnpm run ci` gate in the background (foreground `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` runs first, confirmed green quickly; the trailing `expo export --platform ios` bundle step is slow — ~2 min — so it was launched detached and polled via a log file rather than blocking the session; this is a `run_in_background`/log-polling technique, not a real environment blocker, so nothing new for `BLOCKERS.md`) — exit 0: 620 tests / 63 suites passed (up from M2-01's 587, +33 all in this task), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean (one `@typescript-eslint/array-type` warning in the new test file auto-fixed via `eslint --fix` before commit), coverage thresholds hold (`src/data/workouts/**` now 99.13% lines / 92% branches against the 90%/85% gate — up from M2-01's 97.97%/90.24% now that the mutator/previousSets branches are exercised), `expo-doctor` 21/21, `expo export --platform ios` succeeded (`Exported: dist`). No environment blockers hit. |

| M2-10 | done | `a8412bb` | **Concurrency note:** landed alongside a parallel M2-09 agent working in the same tree/branch (exercise cards, picker sheet, `WheelPicker`) — confirmed via `git status` before every commit that only this task's own files were staged (never `git add -A`/`.`); M2-09's uncommitted files (`ExerciseCard.tsx`, `RestTimerSheet.tsx`, `rest-timer-format.ts`, `AddToSupersetSheet.tsx`, etc., plus its own edit to `ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx`) were left untouched throughout. `restTimerStore` (`src/features/workout/restTimerStore.ts`, `createRestTimerStore()`/`useRestTimerStore` factory-plus-singleton, mirroring `settingsStore`/`activeWorkoutStore`): public shape exactly `{endsAt, exerciseId, setId, notificationId} | null` per 06 §4 item 3; `start`/`adjust(±15s)`/`skip`/`cancelForSet`/`complete`/`restore` actions. `shouldStartRestTimer` (pure, exported) implements 02 §7's start rules — Off (`restSeconds` null/`<=0`, defensively `!restSeconds` so a mistyped `undefined` never accidentally starts a timer either) or a next-row-same-exercise `dropset` suppress; a last-row check (no next row) does not suppress. ±15s clamps per this task's own resolution: `-15` that would take `endsAt` to/past "now" finishes the timer outright (clears state, cancels the notification) rather than holding a negative `endsAt`; `+15` is unbounded. "Only one timer at a time" — `start()` always cancels whatever was running first. Notification body text/exercise-name/set-number needed for a post-adjust *reschedule* don't fit the spec's literal public `timer` shape, so they live in a private `meta` closure variable (mirroring `activeWorkoutStore`'s "repository kept in a closure" pattern) that also round-trips through the kv-store persistence envelope (`PersistedRestTimer`, a strict superset of `RestTimer`) — this is what makes a post-relaunch `adjust()` still reschedule correctly instead of silently degrading to in-app-only for the rest of that session (`restore()` also seeds the permission-cache flags from the persisted `notificationId`'s presence/absence, since a freshly booted store instance has otherwise never asked). Permission flow (02 §16.9): lazy request on the very first `start()` ever (never re-asked in-session); denial sets `permissionDeniedNoticePending` (a one-time flag — M2-11's UI reads/dismisses it) and the timer still runs in-app-only; `rest_notifications_enabled` (settings) is a separate, non-warning-triggering "don't even try" gate. `src/lib/notifications.ts` fills in the M0-11 stub seam (`scheduleRestNotification`/`cancelNotification` existed as throwing placeholders; added `requestNotificationPermission`): time-interval trigger, `interruptionLevel: 'timeSensitive'`, sound resolved from Settings → Sounds (`'off'` volume or `'none'` choice -> silent; every other combination -> the platform default system sound — custom `bell`/`beep` OS-level sound assets aren't bundled in this app config, a documented simplification distinct from M2-11's separate in-app chime library), body `"Rest over — set N of {exercise}"`. **The one real cross-cutting discovery this task made:** `expo-notifications`' package entry eagerly `requireNativeModule`s its push-token binding *at import time* (confirmed empirically — unlike almost every other Expo wrapper in this codebase, which only touches native bindings when a function is actually called), so a plain top-level `import * as Notifications from 'expo-notifications'` inside `notifications.ts` transitively broke three previously-green, unrelated RNTL suites that render the full `app/_layout.tsx` (`db-gate`, `tabs-layout`, `settings-theme-e2e`) the moment `restTimerStore` (now imported by the root layout for its boot-time `restore()` call) pulled it in — and a dynamic `import()` doesn't help either, since this repo's Babel/Jest config for the `ui` project doesn't lower dynamic `import()` to a `require()`-based interop (confirmed empirically too: "invoked without --experimental-vm-modules"). Fixed at the root cause, not papered over: every real `expo-notifications`/`expo-sqlite/kv-store` call in `notifications.ts`/`kv-store.expo.ts` now goes through a lazy, `try`/`catch`-wrapped `require()` (`loadExpoNotifications`/`loadExpoKvStorage`) that degrades to `null`/no-op instead of throwing — safe under Jest *and* identical behavior on a real device, not a test-only shim (also documented at length in both files' own headers so the next reader doesn't "fix" it back to a plain top-level import). One RNTL suite (`ExerciseSetTableSection.test.tsx`) does exercise the real check-flow success path where a timer genuinely starts, so it now also `jest.mock('@/lib/notifications')` per 08 §5's "mock only true natives via `src/lib/` seams" convention (mirroring its pre-existing `jest.mock('@/lib/haptics')`) — this was also the point two of that file's direct `<ConnectedSetRow>` renders (predating this task) were caught missing the newly-required `exerciseId`/`exerciseName`/`restSeconds`/`nextSetType`/`setNumber` props, a genuine `tsc --noEmit` gap, fixed by passing them through explicitly rather than making the new props optional. `src/lib/kv-store.ts` (`KvStore` interface + `createMemoryKvStore()`, Jest-safe default) / `kv-store.expo.ts` (real `expo-sqlite/kv-store`-backed device impl, same lazy-require pattern) persist the active timer under kv key `active_timer` (06 §4.3) — wired into `app/_layout.tsx` right after `activeWorkoutStore`'s own `rehydrate()` (06 §5.1's "rehydrate active workout + timer" ordering), in its own `try`/`catch` deliberately separate from the DB/settings boot-promise chain (a kv-store failure degrades to "no timer restored," never the blocking migration-error screen). `restore()` never re-schedules a notification on its own — 02 §16.1 edge case #1's "notification still fires (scheduled with OS)" means the original schedule survives a kill by itself; a persisted entry already past `endsAt` is dropped and the stale kv entry cleaned up, satisfying "no timer, no duplicate notification" directly. `useRestTimerTicker` (250 ms, active-gated, pure `now` derivation — no store coupling) is the M2-11 pill/sheet's future ticking seam; reactivating doesn't force an instant resync (a `Date.now()` call during render is forbidden by this repo's `react-hooks/purity` rule, and a direct `setState` in the effect body by `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` — accepted a max 250 ms staleness on reactivation instead of fighting either rule). Wired the actual M2-07 check-flow hook points in `ConnectedSetRow.tsx`: check calls `shouldStartRestTimer` + `restTimerStore.start` (reading `rest_notifications_enabled`/`sounds` from `settingsStore` at call time, not threaded as props); uncheck calls `cancelForSet`. `ExerciseSetTableSection.tsx` threads `exercise.id`/`exercise.name`/`workoutExercise.restSeconds`/the next row's `setType`/the 1-based row position down per `ConnectedSetRow`. RPE cells never register at all — 02 §4's "skip RPE" falls out for free, since `SetRow`'s RPE cell is a `Pressable` opening a picker sheet, never a `NumericInput`. A stale-blur race guard (`handleBlur` no-ops if a different field is already the tracked focus, i.e. `focusNext` already moved on before the old field's own blur event arrives) is unit-tested directly. **Wiring:** `NumericInput` gained `ref`-forwarding (`React.forwardRef`) + `onFocus`/`inputAccessoryViewID` props; `SetRow` gained `fieldRefs`/`onFocusValue`/`inputAccessoryViewID` props (the per-column ref-callback map is built once in `ConnectedSetRow` via a `useMemo` keyed on the already-stable `columns` reference + row identity — deliberately *not* an inline arrow in `SetRow`'s own `.map()`, which would make React detach+reattach every column's ref on every keystroke-driven re-render, thrashing the registry for no reason) plus an inline-timer icon button on TIME-kind cells. `ActiveWorkoutScreen`'s body `ScrollView` gained `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"` — the actual fix for "tapping ✓ while the keyboard is up must commit without dismissing": RN's default (`'never'`) swallows the *first* tap outside a focused `TextInput` purely to dismiss the keyboard, before it ever reaches a child `Pressable`, which would have silently eaten the very first check-tap after typing. **Inline Timer** (`DurationTimerSheet.tsx`, feature-layer, built on `Sheet` — not a `src/ui/` primitive, since it's control flow specific to one TIME column's edit path): deliberately simpler than `useWorkoutStopwatch` (no pause/resume, just one Start/Stop toggle, matching the spec's literal ask) — Start counts up from 0 (1 s display tick only, cosmetic); Stop recomputes the elapsed whole seconds fresh from `Date.now() - startedAt` at that exact instant (never trusts the last rendered tick, so a slow re-render can't under/over-count) and calls `onCommit(seconds)`; `ConnectedSetRow.handleDurationTimerCommit` writes that value through the same values-buffer-then-`updateSet` shape `handlePreviousPress` already established. Gated by `settings.inline_timer`, read live via `useSettingsStore` inside `ConnectedSetRow` — same "settings read at call time, not threaded as a prop" convention M2-10's rest-timer hook set. **`exercisePosition`** threaded as a new required prop `ActiveWorkoutScreen` (`workoutExercise.position`) → `ExerciseCard` → `ExerciseSetTableSection` → `ConnectedSetRow`, following this codebase's established "pass new required props through explicitly, don't make them optional to dodge updating call sites" precedent (M2-10's own EXECUTION-LOG note) — every existing test call site updated accordingly. **Two genuinely-unreachable defensive branches removed** from `ConnectedSetRow.handleDurationTimerCommit` (a `!columnKey`/`!column` guard pair that can never actually trigger, since the sheet is only ever visible — and thus able to fire `onCommit` — while `durationTimerColumnKey` is non-null, and that field is only ever set from a `column.key` this row's own `columns` prop already contains) in favor of documented non-null assertions, matching `domain/text-links.ts`'s own "don't write a test for dead code" precedent from the M2-09 coverage note; `handleChangeValue`/`handleBlurValue`'s own **pre-existing** (M2-06/M2-07, not touched otherwise) `!column` guards were left in place but are now directly exercised by a new `ConnectedSetRow.defensive-guards.test.tsx` (mocks `ui/SetRow` to capture its real props and calls `onChangeValue`/`onBlurValue` with a column key absent from `columns`) rather than removed, since editing that pre-existing logic itself was out of this task's scope. **Tests:** `keyboardFocusStore.test.ts` (14 cases — register/unregister incl. focus-state cleanup, focus/blur incl. the stale-blur guard, `focusNext` ordering: same-row column order, row-to-row, exercise-to-exercise, registration-order-independence, no-op at the end of the list); `KeyboardAccessoryBar.test.tsx` (both-themes smoke, Calculator visibility/press, Next press); `DurationTimerSheet.test.tsx` (both-themes smoke, Start/Stop with fake timers, Stop commits + dismisses, re-open resets to 0:00); `NumericInput.test.tsx`/`SetRow.test.tsx` extended with the new ref/onFocus/inputAccessoryViewID/inline-timer-button prop tests; `ConnectedSetRow.defensive-guards.test.tsx` (1 case, see above); and the integration suite `keyboard-flow.test.tsx` (12 cases, real `WorkoutRepositoryImpl`/`ExerciseRepositoryImpl` over in-memory `better-sqlite3`, never mocked) proving the acceptance gate directly: Next traversal order across two rows *and* across two exercises in one sequence (verified by wrapping `keyboardFocusStore.registerField` so every real registration's `focus` callback also records its own `fieldId` before calling straight through to the real `instance.focus()` — necessary because RN's `TextInput.focus()` is an imperative native-bridge call that doesn't synthesize a real focus event under Jest, no simulator/device on this machine per `BLOCKERS.md`; each hop's resulting focus is then simulated explicitly via `fireEvent(nextField, 'focus')`, modeling exactly what a real device's native responder change would do); RPE is never a traversal stop even with RPE enabled; check-while-keyboard-up commits the typed values and leaves focus-tracking state exactly where it was (proving this app's own code never blurs/dismisses on check); the inline-timer sheet's full open → Start → advance → Stop → commit round-trip against real DB state; `ActiveWorkoutScreen`'s `keyboardShouldPersistTaps='handled'` prop, the accessory bar's shared `nativeID`, Calculator's weight-focused-AND-setting-enabled gating (both directions), the M2-15 stub firing, and the bar's `Next` button driving `focusNext()`. **Deferred per this task's own acceptance gate (explicitly not silently skipped):** zero-keyboard-flicker and keypress-to-paint < 50 ms are physical-device/simulator measurements — this headless Linux machine has neither (`BLOCKERS.md`'s existing "CANNOT run here" section already covers this exact category; formal sign-off is M2-19 per the task spec itself, so no new `BLOCKERS.md` line was needed, only this explicit note). **Coverage:** every file this task authored/edited clears the `src/features/workout/**` 85/80 glob individually (`ConnectedSetRow.tsx` 100%/83.78%, `ExerciseSetTableSection.tsx` 100%/83.33%, `ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx` 95.83%/85.71%, `DurationTimerSheet.tsx` 96.77%/80%, `keyboardFocusStore.ts` 100%/100%); This greedy fill can never overshoot, so its own result *is* the nearest achievable ≤ target for free — no separate search needed for that half of the "≤/≥ suggestions" requirement; only surfaced as `lower` when `!exact`, otherwise redundant with the top-level `perSide`/`achieved`. The **≥** half (`upper`) is a genuinely new piece of design (08 §4.4 doesn't dictate an algorithm for it, only that it must exist): takes `lower`'s own selection and adds exactly one more plate — the smallest weight anywhere in the inventory that still has spare per-side capacity beyond what `lower` already used — which keeps the overshoot minimal; `null` only when the inventory has no spare capacity left anywhere (a fully-exhausted limited inventory). `target < bar` (08 §4.4's own named "impossible, suggest bar" case) is a dedicated early branch: the bar alone is the minimum any solve can ever produce, so nothing is achievable ≤ target at all (`lower: null`), and the bar-alone total is the only `upper`. **UI (M2-08's seam, wired for real):** `KeyboardAccessoryBar`'s Calculator button already existed from M2-08 as a labeled stub (`onCalculatorPress` → an `Alert`) — replaced with a real handler that captures `keyboardFocusStore.getState().focusedFieldId` **synchronously, at (inexact) buttons, each calling `writeFieldValue` with that solution's own `achieved` kg and dismissing. `domain/set-cell-values.ts`'s own canonical-math/display-only-formatting split used everywhere else in the logger. **Settings (`app/(tabs)/profile/settings/plate-calculator.tsx`):** same standalone-route/local-draft-write-through-once-valid shape `warmup-calculator.tsx` (M2-16) established — an Enabled `Switch`, an editable bar list (name + weight, add/remove), and the Available Equipment plate inventory (weight + count, add/remove, an "∞" toggle flipping a plate between unlimited and a concrete count seeded at `2` on first toggle-off so the field is never left in a stray-zero state). **Deliberate scope decision, documented in the screen's own header:** `Reset to Default` restores only `bars`/`plates`, never `enabled` — unlike `warmup_calc` (no bundled enabled flag of its own), `plate_calc` bundles `enabled` in the *same* settings object this screen edits, and `SETTINGS_DEFAULTS.plate_calc.enabled` is `false`; resetting the whole object the way Job step order in `maestro` (checkout → pnpm/node setup → deps → Xcode select → Maestro CLI install → build/run app via `expo run:ios` → `maestro test` full → `maestro test --include-tags=smoke`) is sane. **One P2 finding, reported not fixed (speculative — no way to verify the "right" value from this sandbox):** the `Select Xcode` step's own comment claims "Pin a concrete Xcode version rather than trusting whatever `macos-14`'s current default happens to be," but the actual value used is `xcode-version: latest-stable` — to the best of this reviewer's knowledge that is `maxim-lobanov/setup-xcode@v1`'s own default when the input is omitted, i.e. it does not actually pin anything concrete and will float forward as new Xcode versions become the runner's "latest stable" over time, contradicting the comment's own stated intent. Not fixed here — picking an actual concrete Xcode version number requires knowing what's genuinely available/stable on a real `macos-14` runner, which this headless sandbox has no way to verify; flagging for a human with real CI access to either pin a real version or fix the comment to match the (arguably reasonable) "always latest" intent. **`package.json` `e2e`/`e2e:smoke` scripts:** `"maestro test e2e/"` / `"maestro test e2e/ --include-tags=smoke"` are correct Maestro CLI invocations; `e2e:smoke`'s `--include-tags=smoke` correctly matches flow 1's own `tags: [smoke]` frontmatter (confirmed flows 3 and 7 carry no `tags:` key, so only flow 1 is ever selected by the smoke filter). **Dataset determinism claim:** confirmed `src/domain/__tests__/exercise-mapping.test.ts`'s `computeDatasetHash — determinism` describe block (lines 571–605) genuinely exists and is non-trivial — 5 real cases (same-array idempotence, rebuild-from-same-inputs stability, rebuild-from-freshly-re-parsed-JSON stability, a real mutation changing the hash, and input-order-independence via a reversed source array) — confirms "no new Jest test needed, `nightly.yml`'s twice-run CI check covers the file-I/O/`sharp`/JSON-emission surface that unit test structurally can't" was the right call, not a missed requirement. **Docs commits:** `M2-tasks.md`'s `[done]` tag and the `EXECUTION-LOG.md` M2-18 row were read against the actual `e70d885`/`08e7b95` diffs line by line — no overclaiming found (every specific claim, e.g. the 122/1404 test count, the byte-identical dataset hash, the testID audit, matches this review's own independent re-verification below). `BLOCKERS.md`'s M2-18 addendum reads consistently with the file's existing tone/structure (matches the M2-08 addendum immediately above it) and adds genuinely new precision (exactly what could/couldn't be verified about the `maestro` job) rather than duplicating the Machine-profile section's existing blanket statement. **Full verification (fresh run, this review):** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean, zero errors. `pnpm run lint` (`eslint .`) clean, zero problems. `pnpm test -- --coverage` — 122 suites / 1404 tests, all green, matching the implementer's own reported count exactly. Three sibling route-level tests whose empty-state text assertion depended on the old placeholder's exact copy ("No active routines yet") were updated to the new copy ("No routines yet"): `app/__tests__/tabs-layout.test.tsx` (both the `/` redirect and `/workout` cases), `app/__tests__/db-gate.test.tsx` (3 occurrences — success case + both blocking-error negative-assertion cases), `app/__tests__/resume-on-launch.test.tsx` (1 occurrence) — all four still exercise the real route tree / real repository reads against a real or mocked-empty driver exactly as before, only the literal string changed. **Real bug hit and fixed in the test fixture itself, not product code, worth recording:** a first draft of `FakeRoutineRepository`'s `setFolderCollapsed`/`renameFolder`/`moveToFolder`/`reorderFolders` mutated the shared `RoutineFolder`/`RoutineSummary` object **in place** (`folder.collapsed = collapsed; return folder;`) rather than replacing it with a new object — since `listFolders()`/`list()` only shallow-copy the *array* (`[...this.folders]`), the object reference returned on a post-mutation refetch was literally identical to the one already cached by TanStack Query, so its default structural-sharing diff concluded "no change" and never notified the `RoutinesHubScreen` observer — the collapse-persistence test failed with the toggled folder still expanded despite the repository's own state having genuinely flipped (`repo.folders[0].collapsed === true`), traced by subscribing to `queryClient.getQueryCache()` directly and watching `observerResultsUpdated` fire with the right data while no React re-render followed. Root-caused to the in-place mutation (confirmed once `setFolderCollapsed`/`renameFolder`/`moveToFolder`/`reorderFolders` were rewritten to `this.folders = this.folders.map(f => f.id === id ? {...f, ...patch} : f)`-style immutable replacement, matching how the real SQLite-backed `RoutineRepositoryImpl` always returns freshly-mapped row objects and could never hit this class of bug) — all 27 tests green afterward. Left a comment on the fix explaining the trap for future fixture authors. **Dedicated coverage glob:** none added — `src/features/routines/**` falls under the global 75%/70% floor per `jest.config.js`'s existing four-glob table; scoped-run coverage came in well above it (87.77% stmts / 81.25% branch / 82.71% funcs / 88.55% lines with `--collectCoverageFrom` isolated to `src/features/routines/**` excluding its own `__tests__/`), so a new dedicated entry wasn't judged necessary the way M2-03 added one for `src/features/workout/**` at the start of a whole new logger subsystem — this is one hub screen, not a new subsystem tier. **Verification — scoped, not the full `pnpm run ci` gate** (established convention this session, full gate deferred to M3-08): `npx tsc --noEmit` (full project, clean, 0 errors); `npx eslint` on every new/changed file (`src/features/routines/**`, the workout route + its test, and the three sibling route tests) — clean, 0 problems; `npx jest "app/(tabs)/workout/__tests__/index.test.tsx" src/features/routines` — **27/27 green** (18 `RoutinesHubScreen` behavioral + `routine-hub-model` unit tests + 1 route-wiring test), plus a separate confirmation run of `app/__tests__/{tabs-layout,db-gate,resume-on-launch}.test.tsx` — **10/10 green**, no regression from the copy change. Two harmless `act(...)`-warning `console.error` lines appear during the `RoutinesHubScreen` suite (React Query's `notifyManager` batches a stray observer notification via `setTimeout` outside any `fireEvent`/`waitFor` act scope, most likely a `useQueries`-per-preview-row artifact racing a test boundary) — every assertion still passes, not investigated further given the scoped-verification instruction for this session; worth a look if it recurs with a real failure attached. No new environment blockers; nothing added to `BLOCKERS.md`. |

| M3-05 | reviewed | `9a1d301` | Independent code review pass on `0d33e05`/`8a73eb4` found one must-fix correctness bug and fixed it (see `9a1d301`); everything else checked out clean (transaction/atomicity, superset-id copy, rep-range never-auto-commits, `same_routine` end-to-end test, one-active gate, dead-route fix, test quality). **Bug: occurrence-index recomputed live from current position order, not fixed at routine-start time.** `ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx`'s `exerciseOccurrenceIndexByWorkoutExerciseId` recomputed each workout exercise's routine-occurrence match from `workout.exercises`'s *current* position order on every render — correct immediately after `startFromRoutine` (positions mirror the routine 1:1) but silently cross-wired for a routine with a duplicated exercise once `reorderExercises` flipped the two instances' relative order: each then matched the *other's* routine target, no crash or error, just silently wrong data shown/committed. No existing test covered a duplicated-exercise-plus-reorder combination, so the original suite didn't catch it. **Fix:** pinned `routine_occurrence_index` onto each `workout_exercises` row once, at `startFromRoutine` creation time — the source routine's own (reorder-immune) exercise order, computed from `routineExerciseRows` before any workout-side reordering can exist — via a new additive migration (0003, nullable column, `schema.ts`'s/`manifest.ts`'s own doc comments have the full writeup); threaded onto `WorkoutExerciseFull`. `ExerciseSetTableSection` now reads this directly off the store-selected `workoutExercise` instead of taking it as a prop computed live by `ActiveWorkoutScreen`, which is removed along with the `exerciseOccurrenceIndex` prop threading through `ExerciseCard`. `addExercises` leaves the new column `NULL` (an exercise added mid-workout never came from a routine occurrence, so it must never match one); `replaceExercise` clears it back to `NULL` on an identity change (a stale index would otherwise point at the wrong exercise's occurrence). **Regression tests added:** a routine with the same exercise twice (different targets) started then reordered mid-workout, asserting each workout exercise still shows its own original routine target (`ActiveWorkoutScreen.test.tsx`); and a duplicate of a routine exercise added via `+ Add Exercise` mid-workout, asserting it never resolves a routine target despite the `exerciseId` match (`ExerciseSetTableSection.test.tsx`). Also updated the migration-runner tests (`migrator.test.ts`, `migrations/__tests__/0002-full-v1-schema.test.ts`) for the new manifest entry (version 3) and one workouts-history test fixture (`HistoryListScreen.test.tsx`) for the new required `WorkoutExerciseFull` field. **Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean on every changed file, targeted suites green, full `pnpm test -- --coverage` — 131 suites / 1597 tests passed, no regressions, coverage unchanged in aggregate. |
| M3-06 | done | `706b878` | Routine diff (02 §14.4) + update-routine write-back (04 §2.4). **New `src/domain/routine-diff.ts`** (pure, `PreviousValueLike`-style structural "Like" inputs, no `src/data` import): `computeRoutineDiff(workout, routine)` returns `{material, reasons[]}` covering every 02 §14.4 case (set added/removed, exercise added/removed/reordered, target-value differs, rest-timer changed, superset changed). **Exercise correlation reuses `routineOccurrenceIndex`/`exerciseId`** (M3-05's own review-fix column), never live position — exported `matchWorkoutToRoutineExercises` recomputes the routine's occurrence indexes fresh against its *current* live structure (the routine may have been edited since the workout started, 04 §2.2) and is shared verbatim by `RoutineRepositoryImpl.updateFromWorkout`'s write-back, so diff and write-back can never disagree about which achieved set maps to which target. **Set correlation: plain position-order** (`sets[i] <-> routine_sets[i]`), not `previous-values.ts`'s warm-up/working bucket scheme — documented as a deliberate divergence: that scheme exists for cross-*session* display matching (02 §6/§16.6), whereas both diff and write-back are already-positional by storage order and by 04 §2.4's own "per-set targets ← the workout's checked sets' actual values" wording. **"Replaced" exercise ambiguity:** `replaceExercise` nulls `routineOccurrenceIndex`, so a replaced identity is indistinguishable from "removed + a different one added" from data alone; reported as one `exercise_added` + one `exercise_removed` reason rather than inferring "replaced" from live position (would reintroduce the exact bug M3-05's review-fix eliminated) — documented judgment call, file header. **Notes ambiguity resolved and logged as `TASKS-INDEX.md` ambiguity #14:** 02 §14.4 never lists `notes` as a diff trigger (excluded from the diff shapes entirely, so a notes-only edit can't even be represented as material) but 04 §2.4's write-back still copies `workout_exercises.notes` unconditionally once the prompt is accepted for any *other* reason. **`customMetric` judgment call (other direction):** not named in 02 §14.4's own value list (weight/reps/distance/duration) but included in the diff anyway — `createFromWorkout` already treats it as a copied achieved value with no special-casing, so excluding it from the diff would silently update the routine without ever prompting. **`RoutineRepositoryImpl.updateFromWorkout`** (`src/data/routines/routine-repository.ts`, replacing the M3-01 stub): raw SQL against `workouts`/`workout_exercises`/`sets` + the routine's own tables (no `WorkoutRepository` dependency, mirrors `createFromWorkout`'s established choice); confirmed against `finish()`'s own implementation that "checked exercises only" needs no extra filtering (unchecked rows are already deleted by `finish()` before this ever runs) — no redundant `is_completed` filter added. Per-set targets: weight/distance/duration/customMetric/type always become the achieved value; reps is the one carve-out (`computeUpdatedRepTarget`, reusing `repsWithinRoutineRange` from the diff module) — preserved as a range (inclusive both ends, "6-8" includes 6 and 8) only when the matched original target was a range and the achieved reps falls inside it, otherwise collapses to a fixed value, matching `createFromWorkout`'s "achieved becomes a fixed target, never a range" convention. Only `updated_at` bumps; `title`/routine-level `notes` untouched (04 §2.4's write-back list names only per-*exercise* fields). New `WorkoutRoutineMismatchError` (`errors.ts`) — defensive scope-check rejecting a `workoutId` whose own `routine_id` doesn't match the `routineId` argument. **Finish-flow hook (`ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx`'s `handleSaveWorkout`):** after a successful `finish(meta)`, if `finished.routineId` is set and `getRoutineFull` resolves a non-null routine (a deleted routine, 04 §2.2, silently skips the prompt), runs `computeRoutineDiff` and — if material — shows `Alert.alert('Update routine?', 'Your workout differs from {title}.', [Keep original, Update routine])` before either branch continues into the *unchanged* skip-timer→invalidate-history→close-sheet→navigate tail via a shared `finishSave` closure (nothing reordered/dropped). "Update routine" additionally calls the new unbound `updateRoutineFromWorkout` prop then invalidates `['routines']` (the exact key prefix `RoutinesHubScreen.tsx` already uses for its own bulk invalidation) before the same tail. New prop wired for real in `app/workout/active.tsx` (`routineRepository.updateFromWorkout`, mirrors `getRoutineFull`'s existing binding). **"Not re-asked" reasoning (no persistence flag added):** the prompt only ever runs once inside one `handleSaveWorkout` call, itself reachable only once per finished workout — `finish()` already nulls the store's active workout and a successful finish always navigates away immediately after, so there is no retry path that could re-trigger it; documented inline rather than building unneeded state. **TDD:** `routine-diff.test.ts` written and red before `routine-diff.ts`'s body (31 cases: unchanged, per-field value changes incl. `customMetric`, in-range/out-of-range/boundary rep-ranges, every structural case, superset add/swap/preserved-different-raw-ids, rest-timer, replaced-exercise, routine-edited-concurrently); `routine-repository.from-workout.test.ts`'s `updateFromWorkout` describe block (21 cases total in the file) written red against the real M3-01 stub-rejection test first, then made to pass: value rewrite + `updated_at` bump, in-range/boundary/out-of-range rep preservation, mid-workout-added exercise gets a fixed target, extra set past the routine's own count gets a fixed target, superset/rest/notes copied, title/notes untouched, decline-leaves-routine-byte-identical (`getFull` deep-equal, workout finished but `updateFromWorkout` never called), and all three new/existing error paths (`RoutineNotFoundError`/`WorkoutNotFoundForRoutineError`/`WorkoutRoutineMismatchError`). `ActiveWorkoutScreen.test.tsx` new `update-routine prompt` describe block (5 cases): material diff shows the prompt and Keep-original leaves the routine untouched via a real `RoutineRepositoryImpl.getFull` re-read;
