| M3-06 | reviewed | `3a1bd29` | Independent code review pass on `706b878`/`36a5818` found one must-fix correctness bug and fixed it (see `3a1bd29`); everything else checked out clean (exercise correlation via `routineOccurrenceIndex`, rep-range inclusive-boundary logic, reps-XOR-range on write-back, finish-flow sequencing, error wiring, existing test quality). **Bug: set correlation via plain position-order, not fixed at routine-start time.** `computeRoutineDiff` and `RoutineRepositoryImpl.updateFromWorkout` both correlated a matched exercise's sets via `sets[i] <-> routine_sets[i]` position-order, reasoning "both sides are already stored in `position ASC` order" made that a genuine 1:1 correspondence — false, for the identical reason M3-05's review-fix rejected live-position matching for *exercises*: `WorkoutRepositoryImpl.finish()` deletes every unchecked set and renumbers the survivors back to contiguous positions (`renumberSetPositions`), preserving relative order but closing any gap. Checking set0 and set2 of a 3-set exercise while leaving set1 unchecked (nothing in the UI/store enforces checking sets in order) silently shifted the achieved-set-that-was-really-`routine_sets[2]` down to post-finish position 1 — a plain positional diff/write-back then wrongly correlated it against `routine_sets[1]` instead, e.g. silently collapsing a rep-range target that should have survived. No existing test covered "skip a middle set, keep a later one in the same exercise," so the original suite didn't catch it. **Fix:** pinned `sets.routine_set_position` onto each `sets` row once, at `startFromRoutine` creation time — the set's fixed 0-based position among its source `routine_exercise`'s own `routine_sets` — via a new additive migration (0004, nullable column, `schema.ts`'s/`manifest.ts`'s own doc comments have the full writeup); never touched again by `renumberSetPositions` (which only ever writes `position`). New `domain/routine-diff.ts` export `matchWorkoutSetsToRoutineSets` mirrors `matchWorkoutToRoutineExercises` exactly and is used identically by `computeRoutineDiff` and `updateFromWorkout`, so the two can never disagree; `set_added_or_removed` is now derived from actual match coverage on both sides rather than a raw set-count comparison, which had the identical "equal count can still hide a mismatch" flaw one level up. `addSet`/`insertWarmupSets`/`addExercises`'s own set inserts leave the new column `NULL` (never came from a routine set); `replaceExercise` clears it back to `NULL` on an identity change. **Bucket-scheme reconsidered and rejected, explicitly:** `previous-values.ts`'s warm-up/working bucket scheme (M3-06's original landing cited it as a deliberate divergence) is more robust than a raw position index against insertions/deletions elsewhere in the same bucket, but is still recomputed live from current row order at lookup time — a same-bucket skip reproduces the identical shift, so only a creation-time pin (the `routineOccurrenceIndex` precedent) actually closes this bug. **Second-order check:** sets have no reorder mutator distinct from position-compaction (`addSet`/`insertWarmupSets` only append/prepend as a uniform block; `removeSet`/`finish()` only compact) — relative order among survivors is never disturbed independent of `routine_set_position`, no further gap found. **Regression tests added:** `routine-diff.test.ts` — the reviewer's exact scenario (skip set1, keep set0+set2, range preserved, only `set_added_or_removed` fires) plus a two-middle-sets-skipped variant and a direct `matchWorkoutSetsToRoutineSets` unit-test block; `routine-repository.from-workout.test.ts` — a full-stack case running the real `WorkoutRepositoryImpl` (`startFromRoutine` → `setCompleted` → `finish()`) to hit the actual `renumberSetPositions` compaction, confirmed to fail against the pre-fix positional-matching code before being restored (temporarily reverted the fix, reran, watched it fail with the exact wrong-target values, then restored). Also updated the migration-runner tests (`migrator.test.ts`, `migrations/__tests__/0002-full-v1-schema.test.ts`) for the new manifest entry (version 4) and the `insertSet` test fixture helper (`routine-repository.from-workout.test.ts`) to default `routineSetPosition` to its `position` argument so every pre-existing no-skip fixture kept its old (correct) behavior without touching each call site. **Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean on every changed file, targeted suites green, full `pnpm test -- --coverage` — 132 suites / 1652 tests passed, no regressions. |

| M4-01 | done | `e562bb4` | **domain/records.ts + epley.ts — PR computation engine (04 §5.1-5.3/5.5; 00 P5/P10; 05 §1).** Pure logic only, no repository/UI wiring (M4-02's scope). **`domain/epley.ts`:** `epley1RM(weightKg, reps)` — `1RM = w × (1 + reps/30)`, valid only `1 <= reps <= 10`, `reps===1` short-circuits to the weight itself verbatim (P5), out-of-range returns `null`; kept isolated from `records.ts` as a single-purpose, independently-testable unit. **`domain/records.ts`:** one engine, three call shapes, all built on a single pure step function `applyRecordSet(snapshot, set) -> {snapshot, awards}` so none of them can disagree about what "beats" means — `computeRecordsSnapshot(sets)` (full-history case: sorts `HistoricalSet[]` by `(workoutStartTime, setOrder)` and folds `applyRecordSet` left-to-right, returning both the final `RecordsSnapshot` — today's PR holders, Records tab/Set Records table/History PR counts — and the full chronological `RecordAward[]`, i.e. 04 §5.2's own trophy definition: a set holds the trophy for type R iff it strictly beats the best of R among all earlier sets); `evaluateLiveCheck(historySnapshot, sessionCheckedSets, candidate)` (04 §5.5's live-banner case: baseline = the cached history snapshot folded with the session's already-checked sets in real check order — deliberately *not* re-sorted by `workoutStartTime`/`setOrder`, documented in the function's own comment, since an in-session set has no meaningful historical ordering yet; uncheck needs no undo API, the caller just omits that set from the next call's `sessionCheckedSets`); `applyRecordSet` itself exported directly for single-step control. **Eligibility** mirrors `volume.ts::setVolumeKg`'s per-exercise-type exhaustive-switch convention (`eligibilityForExerciseType`, `never`-guard default) instead of scattered `Set<ExerciseType>` literals — one auditable place mapping all 8 `exercise_type`s against 04 §5.1's table for all 6 record types (Heaviest Weight, Best Est. 1RM, Best Set Volume, Most Reps, Longest Duration, Set Records 1-10 + "10+" bucket) plus the informational `bodyweight_assisted_reps` least-assistance minimum (tracked in the snapshot, never awarded as a trophy — no `RecordAward` variant exists for it). **Three documented judgment calls** (file header): (1) Set Records is **11** independent buckets (`1..10` exact + a separate `10+`), resolving 04 §5.1's self-contradictory "10+ = reps ≥ 10" parenthetical against this task's own "1-10 + 10+" text and 04 §5.6's acceptance box, both of which read as 11 distinct buckets, not reps=10 double-keyed into two; (2) `weight===0` excluded from Heaviest Weight/Best Est. 1RM/Set Records (the task text's literal "weight 0 excluded from weight records") but deliberately **not** from Best Set Volume — a 0-added-weight `bodyweight_reps` set is real, common data (02 §4: "+KG optional, empty = 0") that should still compete on `reps` alone; (3) `reps===0`/`durationSeconds===0` excluded only from the record types that actually read that field, not globally — e.g. a `reps=0` `weight_reps` set still earns Heaviest Weight, which never reads `reps`. kg comparisons (`beatsKg`/`undercutsKg`) apply a 0.001 tolerance absorbing lb↔kg float drift (04 §5.3); Most Reps/Longest Duration compare exact integers, no tolerance needed or applied. **Tests — new:** `epley.test.ts` (8 cases: `reps=1` short-circuit, worked `reps=5`/`reps=10` numeric values, `reps=0`/`reps=11`/negative out-of-range, `weight=0` still computes, fractional weight) + `records.test.ts` (47 cases) = **55 new, all green**. `records.test.ts` covers **all 13 named cases of 08 §4.1 verbatim**, one `describe` block per case titled with the case number for unambiguous traceability: case 1 (single-history 100×5 values, incl. set-record[5]), case 2 (warm-up never beats, both orderings), case 3 (failure/dropset eligible), case 4 (strict-greater/no re-award), case 5 (Epley bounds integrated — reps=1/10/11, the reps=11 "still counts for volume/reps" half `epley.test.ts` itself can't cover since that's a `records.ts`-level eligibility fact), case 6 (set-record buckets independent per rep count incl. reps=10-exact-vs-10+ boundary), case 7 (kg tolerance via a real `lbToKg`/`kgToLb` round-trip from `domain/units.ts`, plus explicit just-inside/just-outside-0.001 boundary tests), case 8 (trophy attribution sequence W1/W2/W3 + edit-W1-to-110 reflow), case 9 (delete PR workout -> next-best, both for Heaviest Weight and a set-record bucket), case 10 (live-check in-session baseline: 99->100->102.5->duplicate-102.5), case 11 (bodyweight_reps added-weight Heaviest, bodyweight_assisted_reps exclusion + least-assistance min tracking both directions, reps_only Most-Reps-only), case 12 (duration type Longest-Duration-only + 0-duration excluded + a later-shorter-duration non-replacement case), case 13 (uncheck/re-check re-banners identically + an `isCompleted:false` stray set contributes nothing, defense-in-depth for "finish unchecked -> no record persisted"). Plus a broader matrix beyond the 13: full eligibility coverage for the three exercise types the named cases don't individually touch (`weight_duration`, `distance_duration`, `short_distance_weight`, incl. `weight_duration`'s "duration-independent Heaviest Weight survives a 0-duration set" branch), the exhaustiveness-guard throw test (`eligibilityForExerciseType`, mirrors `volume.test.ts`'s own convention), explicit weight-0-vs-volume asymmetry proof, null-vs-0 handling for both `weightKg`/`reps`, the global `isCompleted`/`warmup` gate (incl. a same-reference-returned-on-reject check), `computeRecordsSnapshot` sort-order correctness against a deliberately-scrambled input array and both empty-history/empty-array bases, and `evaluateLiveCheck`'s real-check-order (not re-sorted) fold proof. **Coverage:** both new files 100% stmts/branches/funcs/lines (two branch gaps found and closed on first coverage run — `longestDurationSeconds`'s "later shorter duration doesn't replace" branch and `leastAssistanceKg`'s "later higher assistance doesn't replace" branch — each closed with a dedicated test, not a suppression). **Full `pnpm run ci`:** green end-to-end — `tsc --noEmit` clean 0 errors; `eslint .` clean 0 problems; `jest --coverage` **134 suites / 1739 tests green**, no threshold regressed (`src/domain/**` aggregate 99.86%/98.38%/100%/99.85% stmts/branch/funcs/lines against the 95/90 floor); `expo-doctor` 21/21 checks passed; `expo export --platform ios` clean bundle. Hit and resolved, in order: (1) both `@shopify/react-native-skia` and `victory-native` resolve via their `"react-native"` package.json field to raw ESM/TS source (`lib/module`/`src/index.ts`), outside jest-expo's default `transformIgnorePatterns` allow-list — extended it (same precedent as M1-07's flash-list / M3-03's reanimated-dnd entries already in that file). (3) `matchFont({fontFamily:'System'})` (used for axis-label typography, 07 §7 "caption/text.tertiary") throws under the real canvaskit-wasm engine (`BindingError: Cannot pass "undefined" as a sk_sp<Typeface>` — no real OS font manager in a headless wasm CanvasKit; the package's own `jestSetup.js` tries to no-op `matchFont` but targets its `lib/commonjs` build while this project resolves `lib/module`, so that particular mock never engages) — wrapped in `useAxisCaptionFont.ts`'s `try/catch`, full reasoning in that file's header; real devices never hit the `catch` branch. (4) `CartesianChart`'s default (`onLayout`-measured) sizing never resolves under this RNTL setup (no real Yoga layout pass) — switched `LineChart`/`BarChart`/`StackedBarChart` to pass `explicitSize` whenever an optional `width` prop is supplied (`victory-native`'s own documented bypass for exactly this — `resolveChartCanvasSizeState`/`getInitialChartCanvasSizeState` in its source), used by every new test for deterministic mounting; `Sparkline` always requires `width`/`height` so it's unconditional there. (5) `useAnimatedReaction`'s Jest-mode mapper registration goes through `react-native-worklets`' `scheduleOnUI` → `mockedRequestAnimationFrame` → a **real** `setTimeout(0)`, not synchronous — the tooltip-interaction tests' shared-value mutations needed a real macrotask flush (`await act(async () => { ...; await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 10)); })`) before `waitFor` could observe the resulting React state update; documented in `LineChart.test.tsx`'s header. **Gap handling (04 §6, "applies globally") verified against source, not guessed:** `victory-native`'s `useLinePath`/`useAreaPath` — `connectMissingData: true` runs `cleanPointsArray` (strips nulls, connects remaining points) instead of `groupPointsArray` (which would draw separate broken segments) — read directly from `node_modules/victory-native/src/cartesian/hooks/useLinePath.ts`; all four components render with `connectMissingData` and a `y: number \| null` point type end to end. Verified in tests too, not just by reading the library: `@testing-library/react-native@14.0.1`'s `test-renderer` only exposes host instances through its normal queries (composite components like `Line`/`Bar`/`Area`/`StackedBar` are inlined away during reconciliation, confirmed reading `TestInstance`'s source — `type`/`props` return `""`/`{}` for anything that isn't `Tag.Instance`), so a new `__tests__/fiber-test-utils.ts` walks the underlying React Fiber tree via `TestInstance`'s own explicitly-unstable `unstable_fiber` escape hatch to read `memoizedProps` off the real `Line`/`Bar`/`Area`/`StackedBar` elements — used to assert `connectMissingData === true` and a preserved (not zero-filled) `null` in the actual `points` array handed to victory-native, and separately to assert `BarChart`'s two-layer goal-met/muted `Bar` colors and `StackedBarChart`'s palette-cycled `colors` array. **Design decisions beyond verbatim 07 §7 wording:** `BarChart`'s goal-met/muted split implemented as two `Bar` layers fed mutually-exclusive-null `yMet`/`yMuted` arrays from the same data (confirmed via `useBarPath`'s `getVerticalBarRect`, which returns `null` — skipped — for a null/non-finite `y`, so this "just works" without `Bar` needing any per-point color prop it doesn't have); `StackedBarChart`'s radius-top-3 applied only to each stack's topmost segment via `StackedBar`'s `barOptions(({isTop}) => ...)` context, not every segment. `LineChartPressState`/`BarChartPressState`: an optional controlled `chartPressState` prop mirroring `ThemeProvider`'s existing controlled/uncontrolled convention (`theme-provider.tsx`) — lets a caller (or a test) create the press state via victory-native's own `useChartPressState` and drive/observe it; uncontrolled (internal) by default, so it costs typical call sites nothing. Tooltip rendered as a `ChartTooltip` Reanimated-positioned RN overlay, not inside the Skia `<Canvas>` — Skia's canvas is a separate render target that can't host RN `<Text>`, so `statSmall`/`caption` typography for the tooltip has to live outside it regardless. **Tests: 32 new** (counts re-verified via `pnpm test -- <file> --verbose`, not estimated) — `LineChart.test.tsx` **10** (smoke ×3, empty-state ×3, gap-handling ×1, tooltip ×3), `BarChart.test.tsx` **9** (smoke ×3, empty-state ×2, color-split ×1, gap-handling ×1, tooltip ×2), `StackedBarChart.test.tsx` **7** (smoke ×2, empty-state ×2, palette ×3 incl. cycling-past-palette-length), `Sparkline.test.tsx` **6** (smoke ×3 incl. color override, empty-state ×2, gap-handling ×1). **Full `pnpm run ci` green end to end:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `eslint .` clean (0 errors, 0 warnings after two trivial fixes — an unused test import, one `Array<T>`→`T[]` style nit); `pnpm test -- --coverage` — **136 suites / 1716 tests green** (1684 M3-review baseline + 32 new, zero regressions), `src/ui/charts` itself 95.23%/78.04%/89.74%/98.92% stmt/branch/func/line, all active coverage thresholds (`src/domain/**`, `src/data/**`, `src/features/workout/**`, global 75/70) held; `expo-doctor` 21/21; `expo export --platform ios` bundled cleanly (10 MB Hermes bytecode, 1 bundle). A `RecordsService` cache entry warmed at that watermark would report a false hit and keep serving the deleted workout's now-gone sets indefinitely — a real stale-PR bug, exactly the failure mode 06 §4.4's watermark design exists to prevent, and squarely inside this task's own acceptance gate ("create/edit/delete a workout → affected exercise caches invalidated"). Fixed by aggregating `MAX(updated_at)` over every completed workout referencing the exercise regardless of `deleted_at` (a delete always bumps its own row's `updated_at`, so its effect is never invisible to the MAX again), while still reporting `0` whenever no non-deleted completed workout remains, so every pre-existing "0 means no history" test/caller is unaffected (confirmed: all 8 pre-existing `exerciseHistoryWatermark` tests, including "ignores a soft-deleted workout's updated_at," still pass unchanged). Two new regression tests added to `workout-repository.records.test.ts`: one proving the watermark itself changes on a non-max delete, one simulating `RecordsService`'s own warm-then-check pattern end to end to prove the deleted set no longer leaks through. **Everything else re-verified clean:** `setsForExercise`'s ordering/dedup/exclusion logic (active/soft-deleted/duplicate-occurrence/sibling-exercise) all read and test correct against `05` §6; `getSnapshot`'s watermark-then-fetch sequencing has no meaningful same-tick race given the synchronous-underneath `better-sqlite3` driver (no partial-commit reads possible); `invalidateAfterWorkoutMutation`'s dedup and full key set (records×N + history/stats/calendar) both genuinely exercised by tests with distinct, non-overlapping exercise ids, not just a trivial same-set case. **Full `pnpm run ci`, run synchronously to completion:** green end-to-end — `tsc --noEmit` clean 0 errors; `eslint .` clean 0 problems; `jest --coverage` **141 suites / 1807 tests green** (141/1805 M4-02-landing baseline + this pass's 2 new regression tests, zero regressions), all active coverage thresholds held; `expo-doctor` 21/21 checks passed; `expo export --platform ios` bundled cleanly. | M4-05 | done | `c6a7490`, `d1cf43d` | **Edit past workout + retro-logging entry points (02 §15, §1; 04 §5.6; 08 §4.9).** Worktree started stale (docs-only, `f131f97`) — fast-forward-merged onto the local `users/tejitpabari/init` tip (`0b8c164`, which already carries M4-01/02/03/07/10) before starting, verified `f131f97` was a real ancestor first (same category M4-01/M4-03/M4-07 each hit per their own log rows). **Retro-log entry points turned out to be already fully built** by the time this task started: the retro-mode logger itself (`app/workout/active.tsx`/`ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx`, M2-05 — `retro`/`startTime` query params, stopwatch frozen at 0 via `useWorkoutStopwatch`'s `initiallyPaused`) and History's `+` → "Log past workout" → `router.push('/workout/active?retro=1')` (M4-03) both already existed and were already tested (`ActiveWorkoutScreen.test.tsx`'s retro-frozen-stopwatch case, `app/workout/__tests__/active.test.tsx`'s query-param parsing, `HistoryListScreen.test.tsx`'s nav-seam test) — nothing to build there beyond closing the one acceptance gap that had no *integration*-level proof yet: a new `activeWorkoutStore.test.ts` case (`startEmpty` with a chosen retro `startTime` at a specific day/noon → `finish()`) proves the persisted workout's `start_time` lands on that exact calendar day end-to-end through the real repo, not just that the UI freezes at 0. **Retro-mode entry-point call signature (for M4-06's calendar "Log past workout" per-day button, next batch):** `router.push(\`/workout/active?retro=1&startTime=${chosenDateAtNoon.getTime()}\`)`, where `chosenDateAtNoon = new Date(y, m, d, 12, 0, 0, 0)` for the tapped calendar day (mirrors `ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx`'s own `defaultRetroStartTime()` fallback used when `startTime` is omitted, e.g. History's `+` menu, which has no specific day to carry) — `startTime` must be a plain numeric-string epoch-ms, parsed via `Number(startTime)` in `app/workout/active.tsx`; omitting it (History's own call) falls back to today at noon. **The real work: `workout/[id]/edit` — the past-workout editor.** New `WorkoutRepository.update(id, full)` (`data/workouts/types.ts`'s `WorkoutUpdateInput`/`WorkoutUpdateExerciseInput`/`WorkoutUpdateSetInput` + `workout-repository.ts`'s implementation) — the literal method 05 §6/`types.ts`'s own pre-existing header had earmarked for this task by name: one transaction, delete-then-reinsert every `workout_exercises`/`sets` row for the workout from a full snapshot (fresh ids, `routine_occurrence_index`/`routine_set_position` both reset to `NULL` — a wholesale edit severs the fine-grained "which routine occurrence" tracking those two columns exist for, which only matters for resolving a *target* during live routine-started logging, never for an edit session where every value is already real, not a target), applies the four meta fields, and unconditionally bumps `updated_at` — `state`/`id`/`routineId`/`createdAt` all left untouched, `WorkoutNotFoundError` for an unknown/soft-deleted id (mirrors every other id-keyed mutator's guard). **Store-injection seam, the actual design decision this task had to make:** `ConnectedSetRow`/`ExerciseCard`/`ExerciseSetTableSection`/`AddWarmUpSetsSheet` all hardcoded `useActiveWorkoutStore` (the app-wide singleton bound to "the" active workout, `getActive()`) directly — reusing them unchanged for the past-workout editor would mean editing a *past* workout literally *is* touching the one active-workout slot, exactly what 02 §15's "must not violate one-active invariant" rules out. Rather than duplicating ~1500 lines of already-tested set-editing UI into a parallel implementation, added a small React Context seam (`workoutStoreContext.ts`, new: `WorkoutStoreContext` defaulting to the real singleton, `EditWorkoutModeContext` defaulting to `false`) and repointed those four files' `useActiveWorkoutStore` reads/`.getState()` calls through it — zero behavior change for the live logger (no `<Provider>` wraps it, so the context default — the real singleton — is exactly what it read before), while `EditWorkoutScreen` constructs its own independent store instance (`createActiveWorkoutStore()`, a factory that already existed for exactly this "backed by the same repository shape, but a separate instance" reason) and provides it instead. New `activeWorkoutStore.loadForEdit(repository, workoutId)` action loads that instance via `getFull(workoutId)` (bypassing `getActive()`/the one-active pre-check entirely) — safe because every granular mutator (`updateSet`, `setCompleted`, `addSet`, `removeExercise`, `reorderExercises`, `replaceExercise`, `insertWarmupSets`, `updateExercise`, `updateMeta`, `previousSets`) already works against a workout of *either* state; only `startEmpty`/`startFromRoutine`/`startFromWorkout`/`discard`/`finish` gate on `state==='active'`, and the edit flow never calls any of those. `ConnectedSetRow`'s rest-timer-start-on-check is additionally gated off by `EditWorkoutModeContext` (02 §15: "no rest timers") — necessary beyond just "don't render `TimerPill`," since `restTimerStore` is its own app-wide singleton that a naively-unguarded check could otherwise hijack away from whatever timer the *real* active workout has running concurrently. `ExerciseSetTableSection`/`AddWarmUpSetsSheet` also gained an optional `previousSetsExcludeWorkoutId` prop threaded down to `previousSets({..., beforeWorkoutId})` — the exact restriction `PreviousSetsQuery.beforeWorkoutId`'s own pre-existing doc comment names for "editing a past workout" (PREVIOUS must never reference the workout being edited itself or anything chronologically after it); this added a 6th segment to that query's cache key, which broke one pre-existing hardcoded-key test (`ExerciseSetTableSection.render-isolation.test.tsx`) — fixed by updating its expected key shape, not by reverting the cache-key change. **`EditWorkoutScreen.tsx`/`EditWorkoutMetaSheet.tsx` (both new):** reuses `ExerciseCard`/sheets (picker/reorder/superset/plate-calculator) unchanged; header is Back + title (tap-to-edit, unchanged pattern) + `Save` (no `Finish`, no unchecked-sets alert — every set arrives already checked, `finish()`'s own invariant); meta row shows a static (non-ticking) Duration computed from `endTime - startTime - pauseOffset`, tapping it opens `EditWorkoutMetaSheet` — a deliberately *not*-reused `DurationEditSheet`, since that sheet's `onSaveDuration` solves for `startTime = now - editedSeconds - pauseOffset` (correct for a live, ticking workout; actively wrong for a fixed-in-the-past one, where there is no "now" in the formula — this sheet instead solves for `endTime` given a fixed `startTime`). `handleSave`: writes during the session already landed via the granular mutators (write-through-immediately against the real repo, exactly like the live logger, since none of those gate on state); Save re-reads the current draft, filters out any set left unchecked (mirrors `finish()`'s own "unchecked rows are discarded" rule — unchecking mid-edit means "this didn't happen"), drops any exercise left with zero sets and auto-dissolves any superset group left with one survivor (the identical two follow-up passes `finish()` already runs, applied here at Save instead of there), calls `update()`, then invalidates the **union** of the exercise ids captured at load time and the final set (08 §4.9's own wording) via the pre-existing `invalidateAfterWorkoutMutation`, and navigates to `/history/{id}`. Route: `app/workout/[id]/edit.tsx` (new, exact path the M4-04 sibling's own "Edit Workout" ⋯ menu item — built in a parallel worktree — will navigate to once both branches merge) + `app/_layout.tsx`'s `fullScreenModal` Stack.Screen entry, same convention as `workout/active`/`routine/[id]/edit`. **Tests — 4 new files, 1 fixed:** `workout-repository.update.test.ts` (9 cases: not-found ×2, meta replace + updated_at bump, wholesale exercises/sets replace with fresh ids + routine-column reset, empty-array clears everything, one-active-invariant preserved during a concurrent `update()`); `records-service.integration.test.ts` (new, `src/features/stats/**` — real `RecordsService` × real repo, since `src/data/**` tests may not import `src/features/**`, a lint boundary the first draft of this suite hit and had to relocate out of `src/data/workouts/__tests__/`: raising a past weight above the current best moves the Heaviest Weight trophy via `update()`; deleting the PR-holder via `softDelete()` reassigns to next-best; lowering a weight moves the trophy away; removing an exercise entirely drops its contribution — all 4 are 02 §15's own literal acceptance bullets, proven against the real engine, not a hand-rolled reimplementation); `activeWorkoutStore.test.ts` (+6: `loadForEdit` load/not-found/soft-deleted/granular-mutators-work, the one-active-invariant RNTL-adjacent store-level case, retro-lands-on-chosen-day); `EditWorkoutScreen.test.tsx` (new, expanded 5 -> 18 cases across two coverage passes, see below: loads+renders, not-found empty state, recheck-never-starts-a-rest-timer even with a configured `restSeconds`, Save calls `update()` with the filtered content + navigates, and — the literal 02 §15 acceptance line — editing a past workout while a genuinely separate active workout is running leaves that active workout, and the edited workout's own `state`, both completely untouched; plus title-edit-commits-on-blur, the meta sheet's write-through save, add-exercise plain/with-Superset-toggle, Replace/Reorder/Add-to-Superset/Remove-Exercise all via the real ⋯ menu, the Back chevron, meta-sheet-scrim-dismiss-without-saving, Save correctly carrying a surviving 2+ member superset group through to `update()` while dropping a would-be group of one, the repo `update()` rejection/Alert catch path, and the `loadForEdit`-rejects defensive path). **Coverage-gap follow-up commit (`d1cf43d`):** the first `pnpm test -- --coverage` pass flagged both new files short of `src/features/workout/**`'s 85%-lines/80%-branches threshold on branches only (`EditWorkoutMetaSheet.tsx` 8.69%, `EditWorkoutScreen.tsx` 51.8%) — closed by a new `EditWorkoutMetaSheet.test.tsx` (12 cases: both-theme smoke, full seeding incl. a null-`endTime` fallback, Save's combined start/end computation, out-of-range clamping, the year-field-cleared fallback branch, cleared-duration fallback, and all three visible-transition cases) plus the `EditWorkoutScreen.test.tsx` expansion just listed; a second `pnpm test -- --coverage` run afterward confirmed both files now clear the threshold (`EditWorkoutMetaSheet.tsx` 82.6% branches, `EditWorkoutScreen.tsx` 80.72% branches) with zero `coverage threshold ... not met` lines anywhere in the run. **Full suite run synchronously to completion (foreground) three times** — `pnpm test` (151 suites / 1943 tests green, final state after both commits), and `pnpm test -- --coverage` twice (before and after the coverage-gap commit, confirming the fix); `pnpm run typecheck`/`pnpm run lint` both clean after every commit. (2) `ConnectedSetRow.tsx`'s M4-10 live-PR-banner check ran unconditionally on every set check, including inside `EditWorkoutScreen`'s own editor — gated only for the rest timer (`isEditMode`), not for this, despite `workoutStoreContext.ts`'s own doc comment claiming the rest timer was "the *only*" per-check behavior that differs in edit mode. Gated behind `!isEditMode`, mirroring the rest-timer guard immediately above it, and corrected the now-false "only" claim in `workoutStoreContext.ts`'s header (`82af528`). | M5-01 | done | `59dea75` | **MeasurementRepository + integration suite (05 §3.4/§6/§8; 04 §6.1).** New `src/data/measurements/` (`types.ts`, `errors.ts`, `measurement-repository.ts`, `__tests__/measurement-repository.test.ts`), following the exact `SqliteDriver`-surface repository pattern `SettingsRepository`/`WorkoutRepositoryImpl` established. **Schema already existed** — `body_measurements`/`progress_photos` (05 §3.4) landed as part of the M1-01 full-v1-schema migration bundle (`0001_exercises_workouts_routines_measurements.sql`) with its own fixture/constraint tests already in `src/data/migrations/__tests__/`; no new migration needed for this task. **upsert(date, fields)**: merges only keys **present and non-null** in `fields` via a dynamic column list — an absent key or an explicit `null` is left completely untouched (not treated as "clear"), which is the literal reading of 04 §6.1's "merge non-null fields" and is what makes the named acceptance case work (second weight-only save keeps an earlier waist value; explicit-null-in-payload case also tested). A call with zero present-non-null fields is a pure no-op — never creates an empty row. **clearField** explicitly nulls one field, idempotent no-op if the date has no row. **Row auto-deletion** (`clearField`/`deletePhoto` only, via shared private `pruneRowIfEmpty`): once a date's row has every field `NULL` and zero photos, it's deleted outright. **list/photos** take inclusive `{start?, end?}` `'YYYY-MM-DD'` bounds (deliberately inclusive-inclusive, not `WorkoutDatesRange`'s inclusive-start/exclusive-end epoch-ms convention — a date key has no time-of-day component, so both bounds inclusive is unambiguous, documented in `types.ts`). **series(field, range)** returns only dates where the field is non-null, ordered ascending — sparse data's gaps are structurally preserved (missing dates just don't appear), never interpolated/zero-filled. **Photo file lifecycle (`addPhoto`/`deletePhoto`) deviation, deliberate:** 05 §8 frames file deletion as "repository responsibility," but `src/data/**` cannot import `src/lib/**` (06 §2, lint-enforced `import/no-restricted-paths`) — confirmed this is the actual established precedent, not just a doc rule, by checking `ExerciseRepositoryImpl`: it never touches exercise-photo files at all, even on delete; that happens one layer up at the screen (`ExerciseDetailScreen.tsx` calling `src/lib/files.ts`'s `deleteExercisePhotos`). Rather than either (a) importing `src/lib` (breaks lint) or (b) pushing all file orchestration up to a not-yet-built M5-02/M5-03 screen (would leave `addPhoto`/`deletePhoto` file-lifecycle-free, contradicting this task's own explicit test gate: "Photo file lifecycle: add creates file+row, delete removes both"), added `MeasurementRepositoryDeps` (`savePhotoFile`/`deletePhotoFile`), the exact same injected-callback seam `WorkoutRepositoryDeps.onAutoHeal` already established for the identical "src/data needs a src/lib-only capability" problem (that one for Sentry). Defaults: `savePhotoFile` is an identity pass-through (returns `sourceUri` unchanged, treated as already the final relative file name), `deletePhotoFile` is a no-op — real `expo-file-system`-backed crop/copy/delete wiring is M5-03's explicit scope (`M5-tasks.md`'s own `### M5-03` "How" names the re-encode-to-`photos/progress/{uuid}.jpg` step), injected at that task's screen construction site exactly where `app/_layout.tsx` wires `onAutoHeal` today. `addPhoto` creates the date's `body_measurements` row first if absent (photo-only rows are a valid, all-null-fields shape); both `addPhoto`/`deletePhoto` run their file-side-effect *before* their SQL write/delete so a failed file op never leaves the DB disagreeing with the filesystem. New `ProgressPhotoNotFoundError` (deletePhoto only — `clearField` against a missing date is treated as an idempotent no-op instead, mirroring `deleteExercisePhoto`'s "never throw on an already-gone side effect" posture, since a bare date is never explicitly "created" the way a photo id is). **Tests — 26 new, all in `measurement-repository.test.ts`** (real `better-sqlite3` + full migration, no mocked repository layer): every named acceptance case from the task's own test gate and 04 §6 (same-date merge keeping earlier waist; explicit-null-does-not-clear; clearField leaves siblings intact; clearField of the last field removes the row; row survives if a photo remains; series preserves gaps; addPhoto creates row+file+DB row; addPhoto reuses an existing row; deletePhoto removes both DB row and file; deleting the last photo on a fields-empty date removes the row; multi-photo dates), plus inclusive-range-boundary, no-op-upsert, unknown-id-throws, and idempotent-missing-file cases. Photo-file assertions are genuine on-disk checks, not mocked call counts: a `fakeFileDeps()` test helper backs `savePhotoFile`/`deletePhotoFile` with plain Node `fs`/`os.tmpdir()` (legal here — it's test-only code in `src/data/measurements/__tests__/`, not the repository itself, which never imports `fs`). Coverage on the new files: 98.8% lines / 96.96% branches / 100% functions (one intentionally-uncovered defensive line, `measurement-repository.ts:309`'s `pruneRowIfEmpty` not-found guard — both of its two call sites already guarantee the row exists before calling it, so exercising it would mean writing a test purely to hit dead code, i.e. coverage theater; left uncovered rather than added, comfortably clear of the `src/data/**` 90%/85% threshold regardless). **Verified: `pnpm typecheck` clean 0 errors; `pnpm lint` clean 0 problems; full `pnpm run ci` run synchronously to completion twice** — first run hit a **known pre-existing flake**, not a regression: `src/test/fixtures/__tests__/perf-budgets.test.ts`'s "all" range budget assertion (M4-11's own 300ms/2-core-sandbox caveat, already documented in `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`'s "History 60 fps scroll + dashboard range-switch re-render profiling" entry and M4-11's review row, which measured a 1-in-3 to 1-in-4 failure rate under contention on this exact machine) measured 595.6ms against a stray concurrent `jest` process from an unrelated sibling worktree agent contending for this 2-core sandbox's CPU; re-ran that one file in isolation immediately after (14/14 green, "all" range 242ms, comfortably under budget) to confirm it was contention, not a code regression, then re-ran the complete `pnpm run ci` end-to-end a second time with no other `jest`/`expo` process running — fully green: `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint .` 0 problems, **166 suites / 2207 tests passed** (0 failed), all four coverage thresholds held, `expo-doctor` **21/21 checks passed**, `expo export --platform ios` bundled cleanly (7643 modules). reverse-chronological entry list below, Edit re-opens `LogEntrySheet` prefilled for that date, Delete calls `clearField` (not a full-row delete, per spec). **Log entry sheet**: date field is plain Y/M/D `NumericInput`s (not `WheelPicker`/`CalendarMonth`) — deliberately mirrors `EditWorkoutMetaSheet.tsx`'s (M4-05) own established date-editing precedent, which reserves `WheelPicker` for its one named duration/rest-picker use case even though it was already available; all 17 fields optional, unit-labeled per `body_measurement_unit` (metric shows kg/cm identity, imperial converts via `domain/units.ts`'s **raw** `kgToLb`/`cmToIn` for editable round-trip fidelity — verified `kgToLb(lbToKg(180)) === 180` exactly, so a typed lb value round-trips back to the identical displayed string on reopen; read-only contexts — Measures-home latest/delta, chart axis/tooltip, detail entry list — use `formatWeightLb`/`formatLengthIn`'s display-rounding instead, mirroring `history-summary.ts`'s existing "identity in the native unit, `format*` only when converting" convention). Existing-entry prefill queries `repository.list`/`photos` for whatever date is currently resolved, so opening on today (FAB) or a specific date (Edit) both "just work" via one mechanism. Save calls `upsert(date, fields)` with only the non-empty, parseable fields (never sends `null`, so it can never fight the repository's own merge-upsert semantics). **Photo attach, kept self-contained per this task's scope** (full gallery/compare/orphan-sweep is M5-03's, deliberately not built here): plain thumbnail row + Camera/Library buttons + remove-behind-confirm, wired directly to `addPhoto`/`photos`/`deletePhoto`, add/remove fire immediately (not staged behind Save — safe because `addPhoto` is keyed by the already-known calendar date, unlike `ExerciseFormScreen`'s photo-needs-a-real-id ordering constraint). **Photo-file deps decision**: wired *real* (if minimal, un-re-encoded) `expo-file-system`-backed `savePhotoFile`/`deletePhotoFile` + an `expo-image-picker` capture wrapper in a new `measurement-photo-files.ts`, injected at both route files' `MeasurementRepositoryImpl` construction sites — left at the identity/no-op defaults instead would mean an attached photo silently vanishes after the picker's cache URI is reclaimed, not an acceptable "simple but real" photo feature. Deliberately kept feature-local (not added to `src/lib/files.ts`, which explicitly reserves `PROGRESS_PHOTOS_ROOT` as a bare constant for "M5" with no re-encode pipeline yet) so M5-03 can layer the documented <=2048px/q80 re-encode step on top of just this one file without touching either route's construction call. **`app/(tabs)/profile/index.tsx` deliberately left untouched** — no Measures shortcut card added — per this task's own scope note, to avoid a merge conflict with M5-04's parallel shortcut-card wiring. **Real race found and fixed during testing** (not just a test artifact): `LogEntrySheet`'s existing-entry prefill re-populates `values` from the async `entryQuery` the first time it resolves for the current date; without a guard, a user typing into a field *before* that (normally near-instant, but still genuinely async) fetch resolves would have their typed text silently overwritten the instant it did resolve. Fixed with a `hasEditedValues` flag (set the moment any of the 17 fields changes, reset on open and on an explicit date-field change) that permanently disables further auto-seeding for that open/date. Also found+fixed a `react-hooks/purity` lint violation (a bare `Date.now()` call inside the render-phase reset block, rather than the `mountTimeFallback`/`DurationTimerSheet`-style lazy-`useState`-initializer capture this codebase already establishes for the same need). **Tests — 6 new files, 53 tests, all real-repository integration** (real `MeasurementRepositoryImpl` over an in-memory `better-sqlite3` driver + real migrations, 08 §5 "never mock repositories" — only `../measurement-photo-files`'s native seam and `expo-router` are mocked at the module boundary): `measurement-units.test.ts`/`measures-home-model.test.ts` (pure, incl. the imperial round-trip acceptance case and the 90-day sparkline boundary/sparse-gap case), `measurement-photo-files.test.ts` (mirrors `lib/files.test.ts`'s mocking convention), `LogEntrySheet.test.tsx` (smoke both themes, imperial round-trip, same-date merge-doesn't-clobber, photo add/remove, the date-field clamping helper), `MeasuresHomeScreen.test.tsx`/`MeasurementDetailScreen.test.tsx` (smoke both themes, not-found branch, sparse-data chart rendering, range toggle, edit/delete wiring). **Verified**: `pnpm typecheck` clean (0 errors); `pnpm lint` clean (0 problems, 0 warnings); full `npx jest --coverage` run **174 suites / 2296 tests, all passing** (0 failures) across the whole repo, all active coverage thresholds held — confirms zero regressions in any file this task didn't touch. **Not verifiable in this environment** (headless Linux, no macOS/Xcode/iOS Simulator/device — standing note per `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`): real camera/photo-library capture behavior, and the sheet/chart/FAB's actual on-device visual appearance — confirmed via typecheck/lint/RNTL tests only, not visually on-device. No new `BLOCKERS.md` entry needed (same standing environment gap, not a new one). |

Fixed by adding `<SafeAreaProvider initialMetrics={initialWindowMetrics}>` inside the `GestureHandlerRootView` in `app/_layout.tsx` (both gate-state returns), then adding `useSafeAreaInsets()` + `insets.top` on top of the existing top padding token to every top-level screen/header found with this pattern: the 3 tab hub screens with a flush-top title (`RoutinesHubScreen.tsx`/Workout, `HistoryListScreen.tsx`/History, `ExerciseBrowseScreen.tsx`/Exercises — `ProfileScreen` deliberately left untouched, its content is vertically centered via `justifyContent:'center'`, not flush-top, so the status bar overlap is negligible there), the 3 `fullScreenModal` screens (`ActiveWorkoutScreen.tsx`'s header at the reported crash line, `EditWorkoutScreen.tsx`, `RoutineEditorScreen.tsx` — fullScreenModal covers the status bar too, same fix needed), and every other stacked screen with its own header/first content found by inspection (`ExerciseDetailScreen.tsx`, `ExerciseFormScreen.tsx`, `ArchivedExercisesScreen.tsx`, `CalendarScreen.tsx`, `HistoryDetailScreen.tsx`, `StatisticsScreen.tsx`, and all 4 Settings screens — `settings/index.tsx`/`plate-calculator.tsx`/`sounds.tsx`/`warmup-calculator.tsx`). Deliberately did **not** touch `app/dev/gallery.tsx`/`app/dev/load-fixture.tsx` (dev-only, `__DEV__`-gated, no production user ever sees them) — kept the change to real user-facing surfaces, per the explicit "padding fix, not a redesign" scope; no new navigation chrome/discover-tab-style layout added anywhere, per the user's explicit "don't want that" instruction. **Bonus, small/safe per the task's own allowance:** switched `GlobalWorkoutBar.tsx`'s flat `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT_APPROX` (83, a guessed combination of tab-bar content height + a typical home-indicator allowance) over to `TAB_BAR_CONTENT_HEIGHT (49) + useSafeAreaInsets().bottom` now that a real provider exists — equivalent on a home-indicator device, more correct (no phantom gap) on an older device with `insets.bottom === 0`; also corrected `PRBanner.tsx`'s now-stale comment that referenced the old "no SafeAreaProvider anywhere" rationale (comment-only, no behavior change — switching its own flat `TOP_OFFSET` over was judged out of this fix's scope, noted explicitly in the updated comment rather than silently left inconsistent). **Jest infra finding, real blocker worked around, not guessed past:** `SafeAreaProvider`'s real implementation starts `insets` at `null` and blocks every descendant from rendering until a native `onInsetsChange` event fires — under Jest no native module is linked, so that event never fires and the four `app/__tests__/*.test.tsx` suites that `renderRouter()` the real `app/_layout.tsx` (`db-gate`/`resume-on-launch`/`settings-theme-e2e`/`tabs-layout`) would hang rendering nothing. The package ships its own `react-native-safe-area-context/jest/mock.tsx`, but that file unconditionally `import`s `@jest/globals` — a *devDependency* of that package, never installed into its own `node_modules` under this repo's pnpm layout when installed as a consumer dependency (confirmed empirically: no `@jest/globals` resolves from that package's own `.pnpm` entry) — so requiring it throws. Wrote a hand-rolled equivalent instead, `jest/safe-area-context-mock.tsx` (fixed all-zero insets/frame, matching the official mock's own values so every existing padding/layout assertion is unaffected — `insets.top` becomes a no-op at `top: 0`), wired via a new `moduleNameMapper` entry (`^react-native-safe-area-context$`) in `jest.config.js`'s `ui` project. Ran every existing test suite touching a changed file rather than the full suite (fast enough to do exhaustively for the touched surface): all `app/__tests__/*` (4 suites/12 tests), all 4 `app/(tabs)/profile/settings/__tests__/*` (43 tests), `app/(tabs)/history/__tests__`/`app/(tabs)/workout/__tests__`/`app/(tabs)/profile/__tests__` (3 suites/9 tests), `app/exercise/__tests__/*`/`app/dev/__tests__/gallery.test.tsx` (4 suites/8 tests), and every suite under `src/features/workout/**`/`src/features/routines/**`/`src/features/history/**`/`src/features/exercises/**`/`src/features/calendar/**`/`src/features/stats/**` (74 suites/811 tests) — **all passing, zero regressions**, no existing test needed a code change. **Not verified in this environment** (headless Linux machine, no macOS/Xcode/iOS Simulator/physical device — see `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`'s standing machine-profile note): the crash fix's actual on-device non-crash, and the safe-area padding's actual visual correctness (real status-bar/notch height, real home-indicator height) on a real iOS Simulator or device — this is a code-level fix confirmed via typecheck/lint/tests only, not visually confirmed on-device. A deferred dynamic `import('expo-sharing')` was tried next and **also failed** (`A dynamic import callback was invoked without --experimental-vm-modules`) — this repo's Babel/Jest config doesn't lower dynamic `import()` to a `require()`-based interop, the exact same finding `src/lib/notifications.ts`'s own header already documents for `expo-notifications`. Fixed by mirroring `notifications.ts`/`sound.ts`'s already-established fix precisely: a lazy, `try/catch`-guarded `require('expo-sharing')` **inside** `shareFile`, never at module scope (`loadExpoSharing()`, same shape as `loadExpoNotifications`/`loadExpoAudio`) — third instance of this exact fix in this codebase for the identical class of "native package throws on mere import under Jest" problem. **Settings → DATA section** (`app/(tabs)/profile/settings/index.tsx`): added the section the M5-04 comment reserved verbatim ("whoever builds M5-06/07/09 next should add a DATA section here"), one "Export CSV" `ListRow` only (Import Hevy CSV/Backup & Restore stay out — M5-07/M5-09's not-yet-built routes, same "no dead row" reasoning M5-04's own comment already used). `CsvService` is constructed **lazily inside `handleExportCsv`** (`new WorkoutRepositoryImpl(getAppDriver())`/`new ExerciseRepositoryImpl(getAppDriver())`, not a top-level `useMemo`) — a top-level construction would call `getAppDriver()` on every render, which throws in this screen's own existing test suite (self-constructed in-memory driver, never calls the real `runDbBoot()`); deferring to press-time means none of that suite's 27 pre-existing tests are affected, only the new ones that actually press the row. `expo-document-picker` itself is lazy-`require()`d (confirmed via its own built output — `requireNativeModule` at module-eval time — that a plain top-level import throws under Jest, the same class of bug `share-file.ts`'s header already documents for `expo-sharing`). **Real ambiguous-spec decisions made (not silently guessed)** — all documented in the relevant file's own header/doc comments: (1) malformed-row scope: only identity fields (`title`/`start_time`/`end_time`/`exercise_title`) are fatal-row-drop triggers; a garbled *measurement* field (unparseable `weight_kg`/`reps`/etc.) degrades to `null` silently rather than dropping the row, since every value field in this schema is already nullable by design and coercing "unparseable" to the same `null` a legitimately-absent value gets never fabricates a wrong number (`domain/hevy-import.ts`'s `parseNumericOrNull` doc comment). (2) Exercise-type-inference fallback priority for the 10 flag-combinations the 6-rule table doesn't name: `weight+reps` > `reps alone` > `weight+duration`, flagged `ambiguous: true`; exhaustively verified only 3 of those 10 combinations are actually reachable (the other 7 either exact-match one of the 6 named rules already or fall through to a `weight_reps` last resort) by enumerating all 16 boolean flag combinations — documented inline in `inferExerciseType`'s own comment. (3) RPE-snap exact-tie tie-break (e.g. `6.5` between `6`/`7`) resolves to the **lower** value — no spec guidance either way, documented in `snapRpe`'s doc comment. (4) Auto-created exercises get `primaryMuscleGroup: 'other'` — no equivalent signal exists anywhere in a Hevy CSV row; an honest "not set" beats inventing a guess, and the auto-create is already flagged in the report for the user to re-type. (5) Duplicate-detection scope: matches `(title, start_time)` against **every** non-deleted `workouts` row regardless of `state`, not just `completed` ones — conservative, avoids ever double-importing something already logged. (6) Custom-exercise name matching prefers an **active** row over an **archived** one sharing the same name on a collision (05 §7.4 re-link semantics) — `buildExerciseNameIndex`'s own doc comment. **Verified vs not on this headless Linux machine**: `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm lint` clean, `npx jest --coverage` full suite green **192 suites / 2517 tests**, zero failures, zero flakes across the run recorded here (coverage: `domain/hevy-import.ts` 100%/99.33%/100%/100% stmts/branch/funcs/lines, `features/data-transfer/hevy-import-service.ts` 97.36%/72.22%/100%/99.06% — the one uncovered branch is a defensive "no resolved exercise id" throw documented as structurally unreachable in the code itself, `lib/csv.ts` and `lib/hevy-import-file.ts` both 100% across the board; global `coverageThreshold` gates all held). All 24 `hevy-import-service.test.ts` integration tests run against a real `better-sqlite3` driver + real `ExerciseRepositoryImpl` (no repository mocking, 08 §5's own posture), covering every named acceptance case: sample-fixture counts (4 workouts/14 sets), superset preservation, RPE snap+warn, all 4 set types incl. unknown→normal+warn, imperial (lbs/miles) header conversion, one case per all 6 exercise-type-inference rules, duplicate-workout skip (both "the duplicate itself" and "a sibling workout in the same file survives"), malformed-row line number, the all-or-nothing whole-workout skip, and two distinct custom-exercise re-link cases (re-imported CSV re-links to a previously-auto-created exercise; a separately pre-existing custom exercise matches by name too) plus a preview-to-confirm race-condition case. **Not verifiable here** (no iOS/Android simulator, no real document-picker UI, no on-device large-file-import timing): the real system document-picker sheet, the actual OS file-provider round trip (iCloud Drive / Files app), and real-device import timing for a large file — `HevyImportScreen.test.tsx`'s 10 RNTL tests exercise the full phase state machine (idle→picking→preview→confirm→report, plus both error paths) against a mocked `importService`/`pickFile`, not a live picker. Findings against each of the 10 verification items: **(1) RFC 4180 reader.** Round-tripped both M5-05 golden fixtures (`export-{kg,lbs}.csv`) through `parseCsv` byte-for-byte identical to source. Fed adversarial input by hand (embedded commas/quotes/newlines, doubled-quote escaping, CRLF vs LF, mid-field stray quotes, unterminated quotes, no-trailing-newline, header-only) — all correct **except one real bug found and fixed**: a bare `\r` *not* followed by `\n` outside a quoted field was unconditionally swallowed (`'a\rb,c\n'` parsed as `['ab','c']`) — contradicting the file's own header contract ("a `\r` not followed by `\n` ... is kept as a literal character"). Fixed in `38821e8` (only swallow `\r` when the next char is `\n`) with a regression test; low real-world blast radius (a genuine Hevy export won't contain bare CR) but a genuine adversarial-input correctness bug the reader's own contract promised not to have. **(2) Date parsing.** `parseCsvDateTime` is a true inverse of `formatCsvDateTime` — round-tripped under a non-UTC `TZ` (`America/New_York`) and got back the exact source epoch; impossible dates (`30 Feb 2026`, hour `25`, ISO month `13`, ISO day `32`) genuinely rejected via the constructed-`Date`-round-trips-back-to-typed-fields check, not silently rolled over; case-insensitive month abbreviation confirmed; ISO 8601 fallback confirmed distinct from the Hevy-format branch. **(3) Exercise matching + auto-create.** Seeded a real `better-sqlite3` DB with the actual bundled `assets/exercise-db.json` (873 rows) via `ExerciseRepositoryImpl.seedBuiltins` and ran `HevyImportService.preview()` against it directly (not the test suite's own hand-picked 4-builtin fixture) — confirmed real alias matching (`"Pull-Up"` → `Pullups`, `"BP"` → `Barbell Bench Press - Medium Grip`) and real case-insensitive exact-name matching, with a genuinely-novel name correctly falling to unmatched. All 6 named type-inference rules independently re-verified to have a dedicated fixture exercise in `sample.csv` (Weighted Situp/Air Squat/Plank Hold/Wall Sit/River Run/Sled Push, one per rule) with a corresponding non-`ambiguous` assertion in `hevy-import-service.test.ts`. **Independently re-enumerated all 16 boolean flag combinations against `inferExerciseType`'s actual code**, not just the self-report's claim: found the self-report's own EXECUTION-LOG phrasing ("exhaustively verified only 3 of those 10 combinations are actually reachable ... the other 7 either exact-match ... or fall through to a `weight_reps` last resort") is **confusingly worded to the point of reading as inaccurate** — literally, all 10 non-exact-match combinations reach *some* fallback output (7 via one of the 3 named conditional checks — `W&&R`×3, `R`×3, `W&&U`×1 — and 3 via the unconditional default), so "only 3 ... reachable" is wrong if read as "only 3 of 10 inputs produce output." What the source code's own comment actually claims (and what's true) is narrower and correct: all 3 *named conditional checks* are genuinely exercised by some input (not dead code), which the domain test file proves with a dedicated `it` per fallback branch including the `weight_reps`-default cases (weight-alone, distance-alone, all-empty). Not a functional bug — the code and its own inline comment are correct and fully tested; only the EXECUTION-LOG paraphrase of it is imprecise. **(4) set_type/RPE.** `set_type` mapping is definitionally 1:1 (the code checks membership directly against `SET_TYPE_VALUES`, no separately-hand-typed map that could typo); unknown → `normal` + warning confirmed via a real test asserting both the defaulted value and the warning message. RPE tie-break re-derived by hand against `RPE_VALUES = [6,7,7.5,8,8.5,9,9.5,10]` (ascending) and `snapRpe`'s strict `<` comparison starting from index 0: confirmed ties resolve to the lower value for real (not just at the array's first pair), matches the self-report and the dedicated `'breaks an exact tie toward the lower value'` test. **(5) Duplicate detection.** Read the actual query: `SELECT title, start_time FROM workouts WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` — matches non-deleted `workouts` rows regardless of `state` (an in-progress `active` workout sharing title+start_time would count as a duplicate too), and correctly **excludes** soft-deleted rows (a workout the user previously deleted does NOT block re-import). `05` §7.2 doesn't specify state-filtering explicitly ("duplicate import (same title+start_time already exists) → skip workout"); judgment: excluding soft-deleted is clearly correct (deleting-then-reimporting should work), and matching `active` alongside `completed` is a defensible, conservative "never double-log" reading given import only ever produces `completed` rows itself (an `active`-state collision is a vanishingly unlikely coincidence, not a real risk) — no bug, agree with the self-report's reasoning. Confirmed the skip is whole-workout-scoped and correctly counted (`duplicateWorkoutCount`/`duplicateSkippedCount`), not per-row, via the dedicated "does not skip a different workout in the same file just because one of its siblings is a duplicate" test. **(6) Malformed-row handling.** Confirmed via `parseHevyRow`: only `title`/`start_time`/`end_time`/`exercise_title` (identity fields) trigger a fatal row-skip; every measurement field (`weight_kg`/`reps`/`distance_km`/`duration_seconds`) degrades unparseable input to `null` via `parseNumericOrNull`, never drops the row — a reasonable reading given every measurement column is already nullable by schema design and coercing "unparseable" to the same `null` a legitimately-absent value gets never fabricates a wrong number. Line numbers confirmed 1-based (header = line 1) and correct via `lib/csv.ts`'s own `CsvRecord.line` plumbing straight through to the report. **(7) Transactional batching.** Read `SqliteDriver.transaction`'s contract directly (`driver.ts`: "rolls back if `fn` throws") and its `better-sqlite3` implementation (`db.transaction(fn)()`) — the same contract `WorkoutRepositoryImpl`/M5-01's `MeasurementRepositoryImpl` already rely on. `bulkInsertWorkouts` wraps the **entire** non-duplicate batch (every workout, not per-workout) in one `driver.transaction` call — stronger than "per workout." No test previously existed for a simulated mid-batch DB failure, so **added one** (`38821e8`): spies on `driver.execute` to let the first workout's writes execute for real, then throws on the second workout's `INSERT INTO workouts`, and asserts zero rows persisted for *either* workout afterward — proves the already-executed first workout's rows really do roll back too, not just the failing one. **(8) Custom-exercise re-link (05 §7.4).** Section literally says "the same name re-links to the same custom exercise on round-trip." `buildExerciseNameIndex` builds active/archived maps separately and merges with active winning on collision. The existing suite already covers the primary re-link case (`'re-links a re-imported CSV's unmatched exercise name to the SAME previously-auto-created custom exercise, not a duplicate'`) and the pre-existing-custom-name case, but the documented "active wins over archived on a name collision" precedence had **zero test coverage** — added one (`38821e8`): creates and archives an exercise named "Shared Name," creates a second *active* exercise with the same name (legal since `idx_exercises_name_active` only constrains active rows), imports a CSV row referencing that name, and asserts the match resolves to the active exercise's id, never the archived one's. **(9) Bulk records invalidation.** `HevyImportScreen.handleConfirm` calls `invalidateAfterWorkoutMutation(queryClient, report.touchedExerciseIds)` exactly once, after `confirm()` resolves — not per-workout. `touchedExerciseIds` is built via `confirm()`'s own `Set`-deduped `flatMap` over every newly-imported workout's exercises, and `invalidateAfterWorkoutMutation` itself re-dedupes and invalidates `['records', exerciseId]` per id plus `['history']`/`['stats']`/`['calendar']` once each — read directly against `records-service.ts`, correctly covers every touched exercise in one pass, matching the established pattern. **(10) UI/route wiring.** `/import/hevy` registered `fullScreenModal` in `app/_layout.tsx`; Settings → DATA's "Import Hevy CSV" row navigates there. `PreviewSection` sources every stat (`workoutsFoundCount`, `duplicateWorkoutCount`, `dateRange`, `matchedExerciseTitles`, `unmatchedExercises`, `workoutSkips`, `malformedRows`, `warnings`) directly off the real `HevyImportPreview` return shape, no placeholders. `ReportSection` uses distinct `HevyImportReport` fields (`importedWorkoutCount`/`importedSetCount`/etc.), independently confirmed via `HevyImportScreen.test.tsx`'s `buildPreview`/`buildReport` test fixtures using deliberately different numbers for preview vs. report and `expect(confirm).toHaveBeenCalledWith(previewResult)` — proves the confirm→report flow is real, not preview counts re-displayed. **Bugs found and fixed** (`38821e8`, separate from the merge commit): (1) the `parseCsv` bare-CR bug above (P2 — real but low real-world likelihood, fixed with regression test); (2) two coverage gaps closed with regression tests locking in already-correct-but-previously-unverified behavior — mid-batch transactional rollback (P0-adjacent given the stakes of this importer; behavior was already correct, now proven) and archived/active exercise-name collision precedence. **Coverage** (final, full-suite run): `domain/hevy-import.ts` 100%/99.33%/100%/100%, `domain/csv-codec.ts` 98.27%/93.93%/100%/98.14%, `lib/csv.ts` 100%/100%/100%/100% (unchanged at 100% after the fix — the new branch was covered by the new test), `lib/hevy-import-file.ts` 100%/100%/100%/100%, `features/data-transfer/hevy-import-service.ts` 97.36%/77.77%/100%/99.06% (branch coverage up from 72.22% pre-fix via the two new regression tests; the one remaining uncovered line, 385, is the documented structurally-unreachable defensive throw). **Full gate, run to completion in the foreground, twice**: `pnpm typecheck` clean (0 errors), `pnpm lint` clean (0 problems). `npx jest --coverage` run twice in full: first run **192 suites / 2520 tests, all passing**, exit code 0, zero flakes (including `perf-budgets.test.ts` — the documented `BLOCKERS.md` M4-11 flake did not recur on either run); second run (post-fix, for final confirmation) also **192 suites / 2520 tests, all passing**, global coverage 96.74%/88.92%/92.49%/97.1% stmts/branch/funcs/lines, all `coverageThreshold` globs passing. (Also removed a stale `.claude/worktrees/agent-ae176b54511a7f64c` git worktree left over from the M5-07 sub-agent session — `git worktree remove`, no code impact, just eliminated a `jest-haste-map` duplicate-module-name warning during local test runs.) Verdict: **pass, fixed** — one real bug (the CSV bare-CR reader gap) found and fixed with a regression test, plus two coverage gaps closed with regression tests proving already-correct high-stakes behavior (transactional atomicity, custom-exercise re-link precedence) rather than leaving them merely asserted in a doc comment; every other self-reported claim (RFC 4180 round-trip correctness, date-parse symmetry, 6-rule type inference reachability, 1:1 set_type mapping, RPE tie-break, duplicate-detection scope, malformed-row scope, bulk invalidation, UI wiring) independently re-verified against the real code and tests, with one self-report wording imprecision flagged (item 3 above) that does not indicate a functional bug. |

Chose `fflate` over `jszip` specifically for zero runtime dependencies (`jszip` pulls in `pako`/`lie`/`setimmediate`/`readable-stream` — a Node-stream polyfill this app doesn't otherwise need anywhere, more surface for Metro's package-exports resolution to get wrong in exactly the one place — on-device bundling — this machine can't verify) and a fully-synchronous API (`zipSync`/`unzipSync`, no Web Worker/`worker_threads` fallback path to reason about under Hermes, where neither exists) — full reasoning in `src/lib/zip.ts`'s own header. Added `base64-js` (also zero deps) for the `Uint8Array`↔base64 conversion `expo-file-system`'s string-only I/O needs (no `Buffer`/`atob` under Hermes — same "no built-in polyfill" story `data/shared/uuid.ts`'s own header already documents for `crypto`). **Schema-version decision**: 05 §9 describes restoring an older dump by "migrat[ing] the logical dump forward" — this app is pre-launch (no released build, no real user has ever run an older schema version), so there is no actual older `db.json` shape in existence to migrate from; built the real, buildable half instead — `restore()` rejects any `db.json` whose `schemaVersion` doesn't **exactly** match the running app's current `app_meta.schema_version`, with a clear, actionable error, before touching any table. No speculative forward-migration logic was written for versions that don't exist — documented as a known, deliberate gap (`backup-service.ts`'s own header, "Schema-version gate") rather than guessed at, per this task's own explicit instruction to be honest about this rather than build something untestable against a nonexistent prior version. **Files added**: `src/lib/zip.ts` (zip/base64 codec, wraps `fflate`+`base64-js`), `src/lib/backup-file.ts` (cache-dir zip write + arbitrary-file base64 read, mirrors `csv-export-file.ts`/`hevy-import-file.ts`), `src/features/data-transfer/backup-service.ts` (`BackupService.export`/`restore` — raw `SELECT *`/`DELETE`/`INSERT` against all 12 real tables via `SqliteDriver`, parent-before-child insert order / reverse for delete, one `driver.transaction` for the whole replace-all), `src/features/data-transfer/BackupRestoreScreen.tsx` (double-confirm Alert chain using 05 §9's own quoted "This replaces all current data" copy verbatim, then a final "can't be undone" confirm; orphan sweep + `queryClient.clear()` on success — both caller-side, not inside the service, mirroring `HevyImportScreen.tsx`'s own "bulk invalidation happens here" split), `app/backup/restore.tsx` (thin route wrapper, registered `fullScreenModal` in `app/_layout.tsx`). **Files edited**: `src/lib/progress-photos.ts` (+`readProgressPhotoFileBase64`/`writeProgressPhotoFileBase64`), `src/lib/notifications.ts` (+`scheduleMonthlyBackupReminder`/`cancelBackupReminder`, a `MONTHLY` calendar trigger under a fixed `identifier` — `kyro-backup-reminder` — so re-enable/disable never needs a persisted notification id, unlike `restTimerStore`'s per-timer id), `src/data/settings/settings-schema.ts` (+`backup_reminder_enabled: boolean`, default `false` — 10 §9 says "opt-in default on post-launch," and `M5-tasks.md`'s own M5-09 line says explicitly "default off for now, M7-06 flips"; this app has no released build yet so "on post-launch" doesn't apply today), `app/(tabs)/profile/settings/index.tsx` (+Backup row — one-shot action, same shape as Export CSV; +Restore row → `/backup/restore`; +Monthly Backup Reminder toggle, schedules/cancels directly off its own `onValueChange` since there's no other natural "start" event the way `rest_notifications_enabled` has `restTimerStore`). **New settings key**: `backup_reminder_enabled` (21st key, `SETTINGS_KEYS.toHaveLength(21)` updated from 20 in `settings-repository.test.ts`). **Tests — 46 new across 6 files**: `src/lib/__tests__/zip.test.ts` (9, real `fflate`/`base64-js` round-trips incl. corrupt-input, store-vs-compress byte-size sanity, full base64 round trip matching the real `expo-file-system` boundary), `src/lib/__tests__/backup-file.test.ts` (5, mocked `expo-file-system/legacy`), `src/lib/__tests__/progress-photos.test.ts` (+4 for the two new functions), `src/lib/__tests__/notifications.test.ts` (+4, incl. the native-module-unavailable graceful-degradation path for both new functions), `src/features/data-transfer/__tests__/backup-service.test.ts` (9, real `better-sqlite3` + real temp-dir `fs`/`os.tmpdir()` file writes for the photo/zip-cache side, same posture `measurement-repository.test.ts` established — **named acceptance case**: seed all 12 tables + 2 real photo files → `export()` → independently force-wipe every table and every photo file (not via the service) → `restore()` → full table-snapshot deep-equal + byte-exact photo files; plus replace-not-merge, 4 malformed-backup rejection cases each asserting zero DB mutation, and a zero-photos edge case), `src/features/data-transfer/__tests__/BackupRestoreScreen.test.tsx` (11, RNTL — **both confirmations required, cancelling at either point asserted via `restore` `not.toHaveBeenCalled()` — the only function in the whole flow that can mutate the DB — so "never called" is definitionally "zero DB changes"**), `app/(tabs)/profile/settings/__tests__/index.test.tsx` (+8: Backup/Restore rows, backup export success/failure, reminder enable-with-permission/enable-denied/disable). Found and fixed one real bug in my own first draft while writing the integration test: `readCurrentSchemaVersion` reads `app_meta` live at restore time, and the test's own forced "wipe" step originally deleted the `schema_version` row too (unrealistic — a real disaster-recovery scenario is "data is gone," never "the app forgot what schema it's running") — fixed the test's `wipeAllTables` helper to exclude that one key, not a production-code bug. **Coverage**: `backup-service.ts` 96.82%/69.56%/100%/98.33% (one intentionally-uncovered defensive line, `readCurrentSchemaVersion`'s "no schema_version row at all" fallback — structurally unreachable in every real path since `migrate()` always sets it, same "coverage theater" reasoning M5-01's own `pruneRowIfEmpty` precedent already established), `BackupRestoreScreen.tsx` 97.05%/88.23%/91.66%/97.05%, `zip.ts`/`backup-file.ts`/`notifications.ts`/`progress-photos.ts` all 100% lines. **Verified: `pnpm typecheck` clean (0 errors); `pnpm lint` clean (0 problems, full `eslint .` run); `npx jest --coverage` run to completion in the foreground three times** — first two full runs each hit exactly the one documented pre-existing flake (`perf-budgets.test.ts`'s "all"-range 300 ms budget, `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`'s M4-11 entry; measured 311.98 ms then 304.41 ms), confirmed non-regression by re-running that one file in isolation (296 ms, under budget — nothing this task touches is anywhere near the `statsFeed`/stats-buckets compute path that test measures) and by a third full clean run — **197 suites / 2569 tests, all passing**, exit code 0, all four `coverageThreshold` globs held. `npx expo export --platform ios` also run and completed cleanly (12MB Hermes bundle) — confirms Metro's package-exports resolution can bundle `fflate`/`base64-js` without error, the closest verification of the zip library's on-device bundling available here (same method M5-01's own row used for an unrelated dependency). **Not verified here (both logged to `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`, new M5-09 entries)**: (1) the task's own acceptance gate literally asks for a manual on-device backup→wipe→restore drill (08 §7) — impossible on this headless machine (no macOS/Xcode/Simulator, same blanket limitation every prior milestone's rows already cite); the `backup-service.test.ts` integration test above is the closest available substitute, proving the export/restore *logic* correct against a real DB + real filesystem, but it cannot exercise the real OS share sheet, document picker, or `expo-file-system` native module, none of which have a host here. (2) The double-confirm flow's on-device UX feel (Alert timing, whether the copy reads clearly on a real screen) is likewise unverified beyond the RNTL test's own button-press-level assertions. Verdict: **done** — export/restore round-trips exactly (incl. photos) against a real DB+filesystem, replace-all is genuinely destructive (not merge), the double-confirm flow's both-required/cancel-aborts-cleanly contract is proven at the RNTL layer, schema-version gate rejects mismatches without touching the DB, zip library choice is reasoned and its logic fully tested (bundling verified, on-device round-trip not), full suite green with zero regressions, both known verification gaps (manual drill, real on-device UX) logged honestly rather than fabricated. |
| M5-09 | reviewed | `5920d85`, `ed3f096` | **Independent code review** of M5-09 (`dba0e03`/`cb8e5db`/`948c83e`) against `M5-tasks.md`'s M5-09 section, `05` §9, `10` §9, `docs/plan/BLOCKERS.md`'s M5-09 entries, and the M5-04/M5-06/M5-07/M5-08 `reviewed` rows above as the rigor reference. Read every in-scope file line-by-line with fresh eyes — `zip.ts`, `backup-file.ts`, `backup-service.ts`, `BackupRestoreScreen.tsx`, `app/backup/restore.tsx`, `app/_layout.tsx`'s route registration, `progress-photos.ts`'s two new functions, `notifications.ts`'s two new functions, `settings-schema.ts`'s new key, and the Settings screen's Backup/Restore/reminder rows — not just the self-report. **Continued from an already-in-progress partial pass**: `5920d85` (present on the branch before this session started, not yet logged in this file) had already found and closed one real gap — `backup-service.test.ts` had full happy-path and file-validation coverage but no test isolating a mid-restore DB failure's rollback scope; that commit's new test proves `replaceAllTables`'s single `driver.transaction` genuinely rolls back both the children-first DELETE pass and the parents-first INSERT pass together (a junk row inserted after export, whose table's DELETE has already run for real by the time a later table's INSERT is made to throw, survives intact) — re-run and independently confirmed still green before doing any further work, not re-litigated. **New finding this pass, `ed3f096` — a real zip-slip / path-traversal bug in the destructive restore path**: `restoreBackup` derived each photo's on-disk file name via `path.slice(PHOTOS_ZIP_PREFIX.length)` directly off the zip archive's own entry paths, with no check that the result stays inside `photos/progress/`, before handing it to `writeProgressPhotoFileBase64` — which joins `fileName` onto the real photo directory via plain string concatenation (`progressPhotoUri`), no sanitization of its own. Confirmed exploitable by construction, not just in theory: a restore's input file is picked from anywhere on-device via the OS document picker (05 §9's own restore flow) — not necessarily a zip this app ever produced — so a crafted or corrupted archive containing an entry like `photos/progress/../../../evil.jpg` passes the existing prefix filter (`path.startsWith(PHOTOS_ZIP_PREFIX)` is still true) and would resolve outside the intended directory, writing wherever that lands inside the app sandbox — including over the SQLite database file `replaceAllTables` had just finished restoring moments earlier, or over other app data. This is squarely a "destructive restore path, data-loss risk" bug per this review's own brief, not a style nit. Fixed by adding `isSafeBackupPhotoFileName` (rejects empty, `.`, `..`, and any name containing `/` or `\`) and validating every photo entry's derived name *before* `replaceAllTables` runs — same "reject the whole file up front, before mutating anything" posture the existing schema-version/corrupt-JSON/no-db.json checks already establish, so a malicious archive is rejected with zero DB mutation and zero file writes rather than partially applied. One new regression test (`backup-service.test.ts`, real `better-sqlite3` + real temp-dir filesystem): a zip with a valid `db.json` (real schema version, read live off the test's own driver) plus a `photos/progress/../../../evil.jpg` entry is rejected with an `'unsafe photo file path'` error, asserted against both a full table-snapshot equality (zero DB mutation) and an empty real photo directory listing (zero file writes) — not a mocked call-count proxy. **Other areas specifically checked for the bug classes this review's brief calls out, none found**: table insert/delete ordering (`BACKUP_TABLES` is single-sourced, parent-first for INSERT and `[...BACKUP_TABLES].reverse()` for DELETE, so the two orderings cannot drift apart by hand-editing — traced against every real FK in `schema.ts`, all correct); the schema-version gate (exact-match-only, checked *before* any table is touched, confirmed by reading the control flow directly — matches `BLOCKERS.md`'s own documented "no forward migration exists yet, pre-launch" reasoning, not a hidden gap); `last_backup_at` bookkeeping (the currently-running export's own fresh timestamp is written to the live DB via `recordLastBackupAt` but not back into the already-serialized `dump` object that becomes the zip's own `db.json` — traced and judged correct-as-is, not a bug: a restore's replace-all semantics means reverting to a backup's own recorded `last_backup_at` **is** the correct "replace all current data" behavior, not corruption); the reminder toggle's "setting flips to ON before permission is confirmed granted" ordering (`handleBackupReminderToggle`) — read and confirmed this is a deliberate, already-documented decision (function's own header: "a denial simply means the setting is ON but nothing gets scheduled, silently — matching `requestNotificationPermission`'s own 'denied' not being treated as an error anywhere else in this codebase"), consistent with this app's existing convention elsewhere, not a review finding. **Full gate, run to completion in the foreground**: `pnpm typecheck` clean (0 errors); `pnpm lint` clean (0 problems, full `eslint .` run); `npx jest` run to completion — **197 suites / 2571 tests, all passing**, zero regressions (up from the M5-09 `done` row's own reported 197/2569 — `5920d85`'s rollback test plus this pass's own zip-slip test account for the +2). Verdict: **pass, fixed** — one real, non-cosmetic bug found and fixed (zip-slip path traversal in the restore photo-copy step, `ed3f096`), one in-progress finding from before this session continued and confirmed rather than duplicated (`5920d85`'s rollback-atomicity test), everything else in scope read line-by-line against `05` §9/`M5-tasks.md`'s acceptance gate with no further bug found. Both `BLOCKERS.md` M5-09 gaps (no on-device manual backup→wipe→restore drill, no real on-device zip round-trip) are genuine environment limitations, not bugs — re-confirmed still accurate, not re-logged. |
